# Ep02 Shorts plan: 7 rebuilt Shorts for "Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down)"

Myth Juice, long-form #2. Integrated plan for production, 2026-11-08. Status: plan only. Nothing is rendered, nothing is voiced, and nothing is uploaded, scheduled or published without the owner's explicit OK.

Inputs: research/ep02/context.md, research/ep02/brief.md (fact spine section 4, outline section 7, visual plan section 8), research/visual/visual_plan.md (look book), research/packaging/packaging_hu.md, research/niche/niche_report.md (section 4, how we stand out; 3.5, the 7-Shorts rule), the ep02 ledger (research/ep02/script/ledger*.json), the lexicon (script/lexicon.json), the locked long-form script (script/script_locked.md), PLAYBOOK_production.md section 14, and the 7 final Short scripts with their panel verdicts.

The per-Short files (the same text as section 11 below) are in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-juicytea-media/4a181100-5b6b-5601-bf64-5ea13c092764/scratchpad/research/ep02/shorts/:
- short_1_S01-pull-the-plug.md
- short_2_P2-02-walk-to-lanai_S02-three-islands-become-one.md
- short_3_S04-hold-on-to-your-hat_P2-03-hat-pali.md

The Short numbers (1-7) are the scripts' identities. The release order is 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 5, 7 (section 8).

## 2. Distinctness check (PLAYBOOK_production section 14)

No two Shorts share a hook type, an opening shot or a payoff device. Log this table in the episode README before rendering.

| Short | Hook type | Opening shot | Payoff device | Loop line |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| #1 | Premise statement plus an on-screen instruction, with a bath-fixture anomaly | Sea level, 4 m behind a back-turned paper surfer on a wave, chrome overflow and chain on a boulder beach (Big Island, east) | Guess beat, number slam over the moat, then a loupe inset on the stranded figure | "And he has no idea, │ this is what's under Hawaiʻi." |
| #2 | "You can't X ... unless" truism with a turn | Shore level, a paper tourist holding open a freezer in the surf (Maui), Lānaʻi on the horizon | Verdict stamp IT HAPPENED, then a twist slip (NOBODY WALKED IT) | "...and ever since, │ you can't walk to Lānaʻi." |
| #3 | A visible anomaly named on screen (a translucent ghost of the missing half) | 45 km high oblique over Oʻahu's Koʻolau with the ghost lobe and a headline | Guess beat, true-size paper Manhattan slam, footprint draw-on with an odometer, then a hat gag | "...hold on to your hat, because... │ this volcano is missing a whole side." |
| #4 | A destination claim over the end state | Orbit over Midway's reef ring with a lava-fountain postcard | Glass sea revealing the volcano plus the number, then the trail line and the postcard riding it | "So, │ Hawaiʻi's Big Island is headed here." |
| #5 | Spoken paradox, word for word the title | Low golden oblique from inside Midway's lagoon, a line racing out, MIDWAY ATOLL card with a carrier slip | True-size Boston-New York cut-out, vertical plunge gauge, upright-hull inset, ink-draining discs | "That's why │ the carriers sunk at Midway aren't at Midway." |
| #6 | Absurd prop plus a superlative | Sea-level close-up of a paper sign on a striped pole in open ocean | Odometer ride down the pole, rename-tag flip, time-lapse, -ISH sticker | "Which means... │ this is the most optimistic sign in Hawaiʻi." |
| #7 | An impossible claim about one named beach | 600 m aerial over Punaluʻu with a giant paper honu and a postcard | Slice plus Everest lift, paper tape measure to 56,000, Everest stack | "Somebody tell that turtle: │ this beach is on a mountain..." |

**Overlaps that remain, and why they are accepted**
- **#1 and #7** both end on an oblivious beach creature, and both titles say "mountain". They are 11 days apart; #1 owns position (more than halfway up) and the moat, #7 owns height and the true base. #1's fallback title, "What Hawaiʻi's Surfers Can't See", is kept only as a fallback, because #2's title starts "You Can't..." the next day.
- **#4 and #5** both open on Midway with a postcard. The cameras differ (an orbit from above against a low oblique along a line) and so do the cards (lava photo against carrier slip). They are 4 days apart (D+5 and D+9), not 2.
- **#1 and #6** both start at sea level in open water with a paper object. #1 pulls back to a wide flank; #6 rides down a pole. Released a week apart.
- **"Drain the ..."** is spoken in #3, #5 and #6. It is the series verb, never in a title.
- **The paper tourist** appears in #2 (main) and #3 (map-scale cameo): the recurring character (niche report 4.6), never near a grave.

## 3. Integrator decisions (cross-Short fixes)

1. **#2's bath plug became a freezer.** #1 (D0) and #2 (D+1) both opened at a shoreline with a paper figure and a bath plug on a chain; two plug Shorts on consecutive days break the distinctness rule. "Pull the plug on the ocean" is also the farmed clone premise (23M, 10M and copies). #2 now says "Unless you put some ocean in the freezer." and "The Ice Age did this for real.": the honest cartoon of the real mechanism (water stored as ice on land; C12-04), which closes #2's VERIFY on the figurative plug wording and gives the loop a rhyme ("the ice melted" into "freezer"). Every beat, camera and loop mechanic is kept. #1 keeps the plug alone. Fallback: the owner can ask for the plug version back (then #1 needs another device).
2. **#5's "Two have never been found." became "Two more are down there somewhere."** Ledger C5-13 bans "never found" and "still lost" for Sōryū and Hiryū. On screen NEVER / FOUND became SOMEWHERE; the description now says "No discovery of Sōryū or Hiryū has been reported." The Akagi depth in the description now uses the ledger's C5-14 wording ("more than 17,000 feet") instead of a specific number.
3. **No Short reuses a long-form line.** An n-gram check of all 7 narrations against script_locked.md found two verbatim long-form sentences in #7 and one near-verbatim in #1. Changed: #7 "Like a bowling ball on a mattress." became "Bowling ball, meet mattress."; #7 "Measured from its true base, Mauna Loa is about..." became "From its true base, Mauna Loa stands about..."; #1 "Put the ocean back." (long-form "Now put the ocean back.") became "Fill it back up." Ledger fact wording ("more than a hundred miles", "about twenty-eight million years old", "from its true base", "most of this mountain is underwater", "more than three miles down") stays identical on purpose. One echo is accepted and flagged: #3 says "hold on to your hat" (the long-form's Pali gag line, s029) inside a new loop sentence with a new gag.
4. **One caption style for all 7** (section 4). #2 and #7 had none; #3, #5 and #6 sat 18-25 px higher than the owner's setting. All now use the owner's centre y 1515, with one question to the owner (section 10).
5. **ʻOkina glyph.** Barlow Condensed 800 draws U+02BB as a backtick (swipe03/okina_big.png). #1 (HAWAIʻI kicker), #2 (LĀNAʻI, MOLOKAʻI slips) and #4 (KAUAʻI label) use Barlow. House rule: the text stays U+02BB; the Shorts' type layer draws Barlow's U+2018 glyph in its place (opt-in flag, so the long-form is untouched), or the string is set in Anton.
6. **Pronunciation unified with lexicon.json:** #2 sends respellings (lah-NAH-ee, moh-loh-KAH-ee) in the TTS text instead of an alias dictionary, and never "MOW-ee" for Maui; #5 Akagi "ah-KAH-ghee" and Papahānaumokuākea "...koo-ah-KEH-ah"; Hawaiʻi "hah-WHY-ee" (w, not v) everywhere.
7. **TTS settings:** every Short starts from the locked long-form settings (`"settings": {}`), so Rodney sounds the same in the Shorts and the film. The writers' 0.35/0.4 (#1, #3, #4) and the solemn 0.6/0 (#5) become A/B takes picked by ear.
8. **Text at x ≤ 849** (channel rule): #2's slips and stamp were moved inside.
9. **Release order:** #6 moved to D+7 and #5 to D+9, so the two Midway Shorts are not 2 days apart.
10. **Postcard claim corrected:** the postcard opens and closes #4, #5 and #7 only. #2 uses the paper name slip with the stamp and #6 a sign in postcard paper; #1 and #3 open on a headline. The shared brand marks across all 7 are the paper language (off-white #F6F1E4 cut-outs with a warm edge) and the paper-flap sound motif. The packaging lab found postcards on frame 1 read as "travel"; each card here carries a non-travel signal (lava, a carrier, EVEREST).

## 4. House spec for all 7 Shorts

**Format.** 1080x1920, 30 fps CFR, rendered natively (never cropped from the long-form). 1440x2560 only after the compression test passes (PLAYBOOK_production section 15). Length 25-30 s per Short.

**Safe area.** All text at x ≤ 849 (QC hard limit 900) and inside the top 80% (y ≤ 1535), except captions. Nothing that matters at x ≥ 918 (the Shorts button rail) or y ≥ 1536 (title and UI). Every cap height ≥ 77 px (4% of 1920). Headline ≤ 4 words in the upper third on frame 0 (#1, #3, #4, #6 headline or sign; #2, #5, #7 a paper slip or card).

**Type.** Anton for headlines, numbers, captions and any string with an ʻokina; Barlow Condensed 800 for labels (with the ʻokina glyph rule above). Load both the latin and latin-ext font subsets (kahakō vowels and Ō live in latin-ext). White #F4F6FA with a navy #0B1D28 stroke of 0.13 x cap, or ink #14202A-#1E1A16 on paper #F6F1E4. No black caption boxes, no accent-word boxes, no curved arrows, no lone dashed sea-level line, no tan drained basin (gate.py: tan ≤ 10% of every drained frame).

**Captions (burned in, all 7).**
- Anton, cap 80 (4.2% of frame height), ALL CAPS, 1-3 words per card, white with the navy stroke, one active word in cyan #00E5FF, no box.
- Synced within 0.1 s of the word times (timing.json mapped through the lexicon caption_map, so Hawaiian words show their native spellings); no card shorter than 0.3 s.
- Centred at x 460 and y 1515 (the owner's setting). The widest card must stay ≤ 778 px so it ends at x ≤ 849.
- At cap 80 the cap box spans y 1475-1555, so its lower 19 px sit inside the bottom-20% band. Captions are the only element the playbook allows there; see the open question in section 10.
- Suppressed while the frame already shows the same words (headline, number block, slip, label), and none on the first frame, the last frame or a loop connector word.
- Seam: caption cards switch hard by design; every loop metric is measured on the picture layer before captions.
- Also upload our own SRT per Short (native spellings) so YouTube's auto-captions never write "Lanai" or "Molokai".

**Numbers and claims.** At most one big number per Short (each Short owns a different one; table in section 1). Every claim inside the brief's spine or the ledger, or marked VERIFY. Everest appears only in #7. "Taller than Everest" is never a hook. No emojis. No sources on screen (description only). Never mention AI anywhere.

**Titles.** All 7 pass the niche report's clone-word regex (no drain/drained, hidden/hiding, bottom/beneath, nobody, found, secret, reveal...); checked by script on the final strings (section 10).

**Loop seam (every Short).** Last line flows grammatically into the first; the voice across the seam comes from one continuous take or the take's own pause; the last frame is rendered at its own time (never a copy of frame 0); camera keys cyclic or on a periodic path; every time-driven layer on a loop clock (cos/sin 2πt/L); beds cut to exactly L and crossfaded tail into head. Gates: first/last MAE ≤ 5/255; SSIM ≥ 0.95-0.98 (per Short); concatenate the Short with itself and run temporal_scan.py (no "cut" or "flash"); audio_scan.py (no click at the seam); a phone listen of 3-10 loops by a person.

**Mix.** -14 LUFS integrated, true peak ≤ -1.0 dBTP, voice at least 12 LU over music, comic SFX in the gaps, no SFX motif more than 3 times, loop-mastered (render T + 2 s and fold the tail onto the head).

### 5D. Build order and render cost

1. **B2 periodic water, B1 near-surface pass, B3 loop framework, B9 loop QC** (they block #1, #2 and #6 and every seam). Port from #6's fork as default-off options (section 9).
2. **#1** (releases on D0 with the long-form): Honoliʻi tile, props, surfer; the 0-6 s motion test; owner review stills.
3. **#2**: decide Shot A or its fallback by motion test, then build the rest.
4. **#3, #4, #6, #5, #7** in release order. #7 shares U19 with the long-form's Ch6: build it once with the long-form team.

Recommendation: all 7 rendered and approved before D0, so no Short is rushed between releases. At minimum #1-#3 must be done before launch.

Render cost (estimate, not measured; U5 measures it): the set is about 5,690 frames at 1080x1920, about the same pixel count per frame as 1920x1080. On the look book's L4 estimate (TerrainStage under 100 ms per frame, motion blur about 1.3x) one full pass of all 7 is about 15-25 min of L4 time; the split-depth near pass roughly doubles the cost on the sea-level frames of #1, #2 and #6. Budget about 45 min of L4 for a full pass plus re-renders, plus 1-2 min fetch and about 50 s bake for each of the 3 new coastal tiles. Large data and renders stay out of git.

---

## 6. How the Shorts are rendered from the same engine scenes with vertical cameras

- **Same world, new cameras.** Each Short loads the long-form's own stages (TerrainStage `lab/drain/terrain.js`, HawaiiStage `engine/www/lab/hawaii/hterrain.js`, CineGlobe for #4's whole-chain view, `lab/story/story.js` for paper cut-outs), the same data bakes, the same POST grade and the same UI system. Only the camera rig, the timing and the Short-only assets are new. Nothing is cropped from a long-form frame.
- **One scene per Short** in its own folder, `longform/ep02-hawaii/shorts/sNN-<slug>/` (scene page, camera keys, water curve, tts_spec.json, captions), importing the long-form modules read-only. Chunks render into the Short's own directory with `render_chunks.py --w 1080 --h 1920` (EP_1080x1920); never into the long-form's chunk directory.
- **Vertical camera rules.** The canvas is 1080x1920; terrain cameras are defined by lat/lon/alt, heading, pitch and vertical FOV (listed per Short). Subjects sit in the lower two thirds and text in the upper third; a subject named by the voice fills at least a third of the frame width (paper figures 35-40% of frame height). TerrainStage curves geometry by d²/2R, so anything compared in height (#7's Everest, the tape) is in-scene geometry, and every 2D tag is placed with S.project().
- **Loop rendering.** Frames are rendered at t = i/30 for i = 0..N-1 with periodic layers on t mod L; the last frame is rendered at its own time; #2's Shot A is one continuous render placed at both ends; #3, #4 and #5 run their seam shots as deterministic functions of t mod L; #1 and #7 bring every camera channel to zero velocity at L.
- **Post and UI.** The shared POST grade; the UI layer (text, captions, labels, numbers) drawn after POST so it stays crisp; captions burned in from timing.json through caption_map.
- **Audio.** Per-Short mix (mix.py), beds cut to exactly L, loop-mastered; music from the long-form palette or music.py, conformed so N bars land exactly on L.
- **Encode and hand-off.** A 1080x1920 master per the playbook's delivery chain (x264, 30 fps CFR, BT.709, AAC 48 kHz, equal A/V lengths, AAC priming trimmed), plus the SRT and a metadata.md per Short (title, description, tags, pinned comment, Related Video). The owner uploads.

## 7. TTS spec entries for Rodney

**Common to all 7**
- Voice "Rodney - Clear Educator", voice_id `eY2VJs4Gi8QKG2RV01sS`, model `eleven_v4`. Pass the voice in the spec AND with `--voice` (tts.py still defaults to Brian).
- `"settings": {}` = the locked long-form settings, so the voice matches the film. A/B variants go in a second spec file with only the settings changed.
- `"lead_in": 0`. Alternate takes use the `"take"` field (it changes the cache hash). The key comes only from ELEVENLABS_API_KEY; it is never printed or written.
- No break or bracket tags. Numbers spelled out. Hawaiian words as the lexicon's `tts` respellings; never U+02BB or kahakō in TTS text until a test render passes.
- Generate only after the owner OKs the texts. Before a monetised upload, confirm the paid ElevenLabs plan with the owner (voice and music.py).
- After every run: vo_fix.py on stray bursts; asr_check.py (small.en and medium.en) through caption_map; a phone-speaker listen by a person.
- Each Short's spec lives in its own folder; the long-form's `script/tts_spec.json` is never edited.

 ]}

## 8. Release schedule relative to the long-form launch

| Day | Short | Why here |
|---|---|---|
| D0 | Long-form first, then **#1** 10-30 min later, same hour | The Short closest to the film's premise ("Look Down") and to its frame 1 (NO OCEAN). It opens "why is there a moat?", which the film answers, and spends no payoff. It gives the new film a Related Video feeder on day one. |
| D+1 | **#2** | The film's first hero beat (the Maui Nui cold open), a beat Hawaii viewers ask for (the top comment on Signore Galilei's 3.04M Hawaii video, 2.1K likes, asks about the Ice Age -400 ft). A new place and zero numbers right after #1. |
| D+3 | **#3** | Chapter 2, a new island (Oʻahu), a live host interest (Hōlei Sea Arch collapse Shorts at 1.6M and 371K). It falls on the long-form's 72-hour stall check: a Short is not a packaging change, but log its go-live time beside that reading. |
| D+5 | **#4** | The time-machine chapters; the niche report's "Midway reef cap on a sunken volcano" funnel slot. |
| D+7 | **#6** | The newborn volcano (Ch6), the comic one; it separates the two Midway Shorts. |
| D+9 | **#5** | The solemn Midway Short (Ch5), a place with living memory (family-story comments); 4 days after #4's Midway opening. |
| D+11 | **#7** | The payoff Short: it spends the film's 56,000 ft, so it waits until the film has had more than a week in Suggested and Browse (never before D+7). |

Reasoning:
- One Short about every 2 days, with D+1 as the exception that rides the launch. 7 Shorts in 12 days fits the plan of 14 Shorts a month at two films a month (niche report 3.5).
- The order follows the film's chapters where it can (Ch1, Ch2, Ch4-5, Ch6) and spends the payoff last. The two exceptions (#6 before #5; #2 the day after #1) keep look-alike openings apart.
- All 7 are out by D+11, three days before the day-14 reading of the Related Shorts share of long-form views. If that share stays under 2% after 3 films, keep 7 Shorts as a subscriber engine and move time into long-form packaging (niche report 3.5).
- Upload each Short in the same clock hour as the long-form launch (the owner picks the hour; US viewers gave 43% of ep01's watch time).
- Each Short: Related Video set to the long-form; the long-form title quoted in the description; pinned comment within 5 minutes. All of it is done by the owner, or only after his explicit OK. We upload, schedule and change nothing on the channel.
- Per-Short readings at 48 h and day 7: views, average percentage viewed (over 100% means loops), viewed-vs-swiped-away, subscribers gained, Related Video taps. A Short that passes 1M makes its topic a long-form candidate.

---

## 9. The long-form does not change

- No long-form line, shot, camera, cue, timing, caption, title, thumbnail, A/B arm or file is edited because of the Shorts. The launch title stays "Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down)", frame 1 stays the NO OCEAN shot, and the locked script (script_locked.md) and its tts_spec.json stay as they are.
- The Shorts read long-form assets read-only (data bakes, tiles, cut-outs, photos, SFX, type) and copy anything they modify. They render into their own folders and chunk directories. No long-form frame is cropped or reused as footage.
- No Short reuses a long-form narration line (n-gram check, section 3); the facts and their ledger wording stay consistent with the film so a viewer who taps through hears the same facts.
- Engine changes the Shorts need land so that the long-form renders exactly as before:
  - B1/B2 (near-surface pass, periodic water) and B7 (tts.py loop flag) are default-off options;
  - B5's ʻokina glyph substitution is an opt-in flag set only by the Shorts' type layer;
  - B8 (U19 slice) is already in the long-form's Ch6 plan; the Short only calls the shared module;
  - before merging any change to a shared file, render 3 long-form test stills per touched stage before and after, and require a match (MAE < 0.5/255). Coordinate with production C, which is editing hterrain.js now.
- **Finding for the long-form team (reported, not changed by us):** Barlow Condensed 800 draws U+02BB as a backtick. Any long-form label in Barlow that contains an ʻokina (for example MOLOKAʻI, LĀNAʻI, KAUAʻI, OʻAHU) will show the wrong mark unless it is set in Anton or uses the glyph flag. Whether to fix the film is the owner's and production's decision.

## 10. Open items before render

**Owner decisions**
1. OK the 7 narration texts before any TTS credits are spent; confirm the paid ElevenLabs plan before a monetised upload.
2. Caption height: keep his y 1515 (the cap box dips 19 px into the bottom-20% band) or move all 7 to y 1496 (the cap box ends at y 1535, fully clear). The playbook asks for a real-phone test of both with the Shorts UI.
3. #2: the freezer (recommended) or the original bath plug.
4. #3: keep "hold on to your hat" (an echo of the long-form's gag line in a new sentence) or the weaker "mind the wind".
5. #1 title: "Hawaiʻi's Surfers Are Halfway Up a Mountain" (recommended) or "What Hawaiʻi's Surfers Can't See".
6. The upload hour.
7. Playbook question still open: does "one continuous camera, no cuts" apply to all rebuilt Shorts? #1 and #6 are one continuous camera; #2-#5 and #7 use eased moves with a few whips or hidden cuts. Recommendation: no (it would make the 7 look alike).

**VERIFY (facts and assets) before lock**
- #1: public-domain anonymous surfer photo (never a named surfer or an identifiable Native Hawaiian waterman as the stranded figure); payoff lines of sight on the HMRG 50 m bake; reef depth at the break (NCEI DEM); drowned reef steps only where HMRG shows terraces.
- #2: saddles and crest on the HMRG 50 m grid; rights for the tourist, ferry and freezer photos; Launiupoko outside the August 2023 burn area; the settlement line into the ledger.
- #3: USGS I-2809 footprint legibility (or the Moore et al. 1989 figure); the tourist card's source record; Manhattan on the block's base contour.
- #4: the postcard and La Pérouse photo rights; La Pérouse coordinates; Nihoa's published site map (no map, no terrace lines); Kīlauea's glow at render time; Midway's visitor status on publish day.
- #5: "The carriers never even saw each other." on the NHHC Combat Narrative page (else the fallback line); Kaga "upright" re-read on upload day; news on Sōryū and Hiryū on upload day; the Yorktown photo catalogue number; the Boston-New York and upright items into the ledger.
- #6: the exact USGS "thousands of generations" wording into the ledger; sill.py and the flood-fill for the islet and the bench 24 km north; the summit correction (or key the counter to the raw grid).
- #7: the honu photo's federal credit line (else the paper sunbather); the bowling ball (PD photo or modelled); a public-domain ortho; the sea-floor-vs-Everest entry into the ledger.
- All: lexicon open items 1-3 (wehewehe.org, test render, a Hawaiian-speaker or owner listen) and the 17 new lexicon entries.

**Checks run on the final strings (2026-11-08):** the niche title regex on all 7 titles (0 hits; lengths 30-48 characters); a whole-word AI-term grep over every public string (narration, on-screen text, titles, descriptions, tags, pinned comments): 0 hits (the terms appear only in internal production notes such as TTS specs, which are never published); an emoji scan of every file (0); ASCII Hawaiian names in public text appear only where intended (TTS text, the long-form title quote, hashtags and tags, and exact source titles such as USGS "Oahu, Niihau and Kauai" and the "Hawaii Mapping Research Group"). Re-run all of them on the final strings before upload.

## 11. The seven scripts, in release order

Order: #1 (D0), #2 (D+1), #3 (D+3), #4 (D+5), #6 (D+7), #5 (D+9), #7 (D+11).


---

## Short 1 (S01-pull-the-plug): "Hawaiʻi's Surfers Are Halfway Up a Mountain"

| | |
|---|---|
| Release | **D0**, 10-30 min after the long-form goes live, same hour (slot 1 of 7) |
| Length | 29.0 s planned (L re-derived from the real take; 28.5-31.0 s allowed) |
| The one big number | ABOUT 18,000 FEET (this Short owns 18,000 among the 7) |
| Voice | Rodney - Clear Educator, `eY2VJs4Gi8QKG2RV01sS`, `eleven_v4`, passed explicitly |
| Format | 1080x1920, 30 fps, 870 frames; one continuous camera, VE 1.0 |
| Related Video | "Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down)" |

All times are estimates. Every camera key, label, caption and SFX is re-timed from tts.py `timing.json`; the take is never forced to fit the plan.

### Integrator changes (2026-11-08)

1. "Put the ocean back." became **"Fill it back up."** The long-form says "Now put the ocean back." (s173); the owner wants Short narration written new. The new line also fits the bathtub framing.
2. Captions follow the house spec (shorts_plan.md section 4): Anton cap 80, centred x 460 / y 1515.
3. The kicker HAWAIʻI is set in Barlow Condensed 800, whose own U+02BB glyph draws as a backtick (swipe03/okina_big.png). The text stays U+02BB; type.js draws Barlow's U+2018 glyph in its place (house rule).
4. TTS settings: the locked long-form settings (`"settings": {}`), with stability 0.35 / style 0.4 only as an A/B take.
5. This Short now owns the bathtub plug and chain alone. Short 2's plug was replaced by a freezer so two releases in a row do not share the device.

### 1. Narration and TTS

**TTS setup**
- Voice "Rodney - Clear Educator" (`eY2VJs4Gi8QKG2RV01sS`), model `eleven_v4`, passed explicitly (tts.py still defaults to Brian).
- Settings: the locked ep02 long-form settings, so Rodney sounds the same as in the film. Generate one A/B take of T1 at stability 0.35 / style 0.4; keep the default unless the owner prefers the A/B by ear.
- Text: no break or bracket tags, numbers spelled out, 70 words.
- Sent in story order as three takes. T3 is generated twice (two loop takes); keep the take with the cleaner seam.

**T1 (speed 1.0)**
> Watch the surfer. Sorry, buddy. The beach becomes a ledge. The ledge becomes a drop-off. The drop-off becomes a mountain. And it keeps going. The last water pools in a moat. How deep?

**T2 (speed 0.92; its own take after a 0.55 s pause, per the key-statistic rule)**
> About eighteen thousand feet.

**T3 (speed 1.0)**
> Now look back up. And our surfer? More than halfway up a mountain. Still waiting for a wave. Fine. Fill it back up. And he has no idea, this is what's under Hawaii.

**Placement.** Split T3's last sentence in its own comma pause after "idea,". The second half, "this is what's under Hawaii.", opens the Short at 0.12 s. "And he has no idea" ends 0.15 s before the cut, so the pause across the seam is 0.27 s.

| Planned time | Line |
|---|---|
| 0.12-1.85 | this is what's under Hawaii. |
| 2.10-3.10 | Watch the surfer. |
| 3.70-4.60 | Sorry, buddy. |
| 5.00-6.50 | The beach becomes a ledge. |
| 6.80-8.45 | The ledge becomes a drop-off. |
| 8.75-10.60 | The drop-off becomes a mountain. |
| 10.90-12.00 | And it keeps going. |
| 12.30-14.20 | The last water pools in a moat. |
| 14.45-15.05 | How deep? |
| 15.60-17.50 | About eighteen thousand feet. |
| 18.10-19.10 | Now look back up. |
| 19.85-20.75 | And our surfer? |
| 21.05-22.85 | More than halfway up a mountain. |
| 23.10-24.50 | Still waiting for a wave. |
| 25.00-25.45 | Fine. |
| 25.80-26.95 | Fill it back up. |
| 27.50-28.85 | And he has no idea |

**Timing rules**
- Speed 1.0 (T2 0.92). Never push the deadpan to 1.05.
- Minimum pauses: 0.25 s between sentences; 0.5 s before "Sorry, buddy." and before "Fine."; 0.50-0.55 s guess pause after "How deep?"; 0.27 s across the seam.
- L = end of "idea" + 0.15 s, rounded to whole frames. Target 29.0 s; 28.5-31.0 s allowed. Music tempo = 3840 / L BPM, so 16 bars land exactly on L.
- Cut line: if L would pass 31.0 s, drop "And it keeps going." first. Never cut the hook or the loop line.
- Trim each take to first character -60 ms and last character +90 ms, with 8 ms fades. Run vo_fix.py on stray next-word bursts.

**Delivery**
- Calm and dry throughout. Deadpan on "Sorry, buddy." and "Fine.".
- A small lift on "mountain" in "The drop-off becomes a mountain.".
- "How deep?" light and curious. "About eighteen thousand feet." slower and lower.
- "More than halfway up a mountain." matter-of-fact.
- "And he has no idea," level and open (a comma, not a falling stop).

**Pronunciation** (longform/ep02-hawaii/script/lexicon.json)
- Hawaiʻi is the only spoken Hawaiian word. Send ASCII "Hawaii" (the lexicon never puts U+02BB or kahakō in TTS text until a test render passes). Target /həˈwaɪ.ʔi/: three syllables, a light glottal catch before the last "ee" (hah-WHY-ee). If the test render gives two syllables, regenerate T3 with the lexicon alternative "hah-WHY-ee"; captions still map it to Hawaiʻi.
- ASR-check with faster-whisper small.en and medium.en; accept only "hawaii" or "hawai'i". It is the hook's last word, so a mangled take is regenerated, never kept.
- On screen and in captions: HAWAIʻI with U+02BB; load the latin-ext font subsets.
- Hawaiian words in the description or production notes only (never voiced here): Hilo HEE-loh; Honoliʻi hoh-noh-LEE-ee, glottal catch before the last "ee" (rule-derived, add to the lexicon); Mauna Kea MAO-nah KAY-ah; Mauna Loa MAO-nah LOH-ah; Waikīkī why-kee-KEE (photo-search note only, add to the lexicon).
- Lexicon open item 1 applies: a person confirms every entry on wehewehe.org before any of them is voiced.
- Map timing words through caption_map before make_srt.py and asr_check.py.

Captions are suppressed whenever the headline, the label stack or the number block already shows the same words.

### 3. Hook

Frame 0 is already moving (the surfer's ride bob, the surf, the haze) and carries the whole premise with the sound off.
- We sit low on the water, 4 m behind a paper surfer at the Honoliʻi break off Hilo. He is seen from behind, so he stays anonymous, and fills 36% of the frame height on the right.
- Straight ahead of him, bolted to the black boulder beach, is an 18 m chrome bathtub overflow plate taking 29% of the frame width. A chrome ball chain runs from it down the rocks and across the reef, through the clear turquoise water, to the left frame edge (nearest balls ~145 px).
- The visual question is a bath fixture on a Hawaiian beach. In the upper third, unboxed: the kicker HAWAIʻI and the headline WATCH THE / SURFER (Anton cap 110), which gives muted viewers the place and an instruction.
- Frame 1 (0.03 s): CLANK. The chain rips taut out of the water in a straight spray line across the left half (y 880-999 at the edge), well clear of the Shorts button rail.
- 0.40 s: a far-off THOOMP, and the whole sea level starts dropping straight down like a tub: a vertical drop, not a drawback. By 1.0 s a salt-white bathtub ring shows on the boulders; by 1.6 s he has sunk ~220 px.
- VO at 0.12 s: "this is what's under Hawaiʻi." (5 words, done by 1.85 s). "Watch the surfer." follows at 2.10 s, reading the headline aloud for sound-on viewers. Two questions open at once: what is down there, and what happens to him? The first gag answers the second by 3.35 s (BOING, he lands on dry reef, "Sorry, buddy.").
- No greeting, no channel name, no number before 15 s. The CLANK and rattle sit at -18 dB under "this".

### 4. Payoff

Three stages, each landing its own beat.
1. **The number (15.4-18.3).** The camera eases into the moat pose as Rodney asks "How deep?", then holds a 0.55 s guess pause. PLUNK: the plug drops back into its plughole and the whirl stops. The last water sits in the real Hawaiian Deep as a turquoise strip that runs out of both frame edges. ABOUT / 18,000 / FEET DEEP slams in over dark basalt.
2. **The real payoff (18.1-24.9).** "Now look back up." tilts from the pool up the whole dark flank to the old shoreline. One frame holds the scale: the pool at the bottom, 920 px of flank, a ring on a speck at the old coast, Mauna Kea's summit above it. "And our surfer?" A 600 px loupe opens on the speck: the frame-0 view drained, him frozen in riding stance on dry reef, the overflow plate high and dry behind him. He hops. "More than halfway up a mountain." He leans over and peers down. "Still waiting for a wave." He snaps upright and keeps riding nothing. It turns "far away" into "high up", and it is true: sea level is 57-59% of the way up Mauna Kea from the moat or from its USGS base.
3. **The callback (25.0-29.0).** "Fine. Fill it back up." The pool rises up the flank as one smooth sheet, a tub filling with no wave front. The camera flies home into the ring in one log-eased move. The water reaches the reef and lifts him back onto his wave at 27.75. "And he has no idea" lands in the last 1.35 s, and the opening line completes it.

Held back for the long-form and Short 7: why the moat exists (the weight and the dent), Everest, 56,000 ft and 33,500 ft. "Why is there a moat?" stays open; line 2 of the description and the Related Video answer it.

### 5. Loop check

**Last line into first line, as one passage:** "Fine. Fill it back up. And he has no idea, this is what's under Hawaiʻi. Watch the surfer."
- One natural sentence across the cut: he has no idea that this is what is under him.
- Generated as one TTS sentence (T3), split in its own comma pause. "idea" ends at 28.85, the cut is at 29.00, "this" starts at 0.12: 0.27 s across the seam.
- T3 is recorded twice; keep the take whose "idea" stays level and open. The second half is a falling statement, which suits a hook.

**Last frame = frame 0.** The last frame is frame 869 (t = 28.967), rendered at its own time, not copied from frame 0 (a copy would show a 1-frame freeze).
- Camera: P0, at rest from 28.35 with zero velocity; frame 0 also starts at rest. Keys are cyclic in value and slope.
- Water: exactly 0 m from 28.35, touching the overflow slots.
- Surfer: on his small wave in the same ride pose. Bob period 2.9 s = L/10.
- Chain: the single authored SLACK pose, used from 15.41 to 29.0 and at frame 0. Sway period 5.8 s = L/5.
- Surf shader: only periods that divide L (9.667, 5.8, 4.143, 2.9, 1.933 s = L/3, /5, /7, /10, /15). Haze and clouds static. Every time-driven water term runs on the loop clock (cos/sin 2πt/L; see shorts_plan.md section 5).
- Text: kicker and headline fully back by 28.80, as on frame 0. No gauge (out 27.0-27.6, in 0.40-0.90), no ring (out 27.10-27.40), loupe, label or caption (gone by 28.93; the first caption appears at 0.10).
- If L changes after the real take, re-derive every period as L/n.

**Gates:** first/last-frame MAE ≤ 5/255; SSIM ≥ 0.95 at 540x960; concatenate the Short with itself and run temporal_scan.py: no "cut" or "flash" at the join.

**First new event after the seam:** the chain snapping taut on frame 1 (CLANK at 0.03). A replay reads as "the tub is full again, and someone pulls the chain again".

**Audio seam:** the overflow GLUG starts low under "idea" at 28.35 (below 300 Hz, at least 16 dB under the voice), peaks at 28.90 in the gap and runs into the CLANK. Copy the last 0.25 s of the GLUG, the music reverb and the surf ambience to 0.00-0.25 (pre-lap). Music: 16 bars at 3840/L BPM, cut on the bar line; bar 16 resolves into the bar-1 motif. The voice is cut at the last word with a 5-8 ms fade.

### 6. Facts (all inside the spine; one big number)

Script paths below are in the scratchpad folder s1v3.
- "this is what's under Hawaiʻi" and the flank shown: spine #1 (USGS FAQ: Mauna Kea rises 4,205 m above sea level and about 6,000 m below); ledger C12-01, C3-02. Terrain is real: UH SOEST HMRG 50 m blended into NOAA ETOPO 2022, with the NCEI 1/3" Hilo coastal DEM near shore, at VE 1.0.
- "The beach becomes a ledge." No number. ETOPO 15" on the camera line (60° from the surfer): 45 m at the coast, then -67 m (5 km), -311 (10 km), -351 (15 km), -619 m (20 km): a shelf about 20 km wide (p1.py).
- "The ledge becomes a drop-off." Replaces "cliff" per ledger C12-10 ("say 'the slope plunges' below the shelf edge, not 'cliffs'"). Past 20 km the floor falls to -1,191 m (25 km), -2,100 (35), -4,251 (45), -5,053 m (50); the steepest 1-km step is 14.5° on the 15" grid. No number spoken.
- "The drop-off becomes a mountain." / "And it keeps going." Spine #1 (about 6,000 m below sea level). 33,500 is not spoken.
- "The last water pools in a moat." Spine #4 (Hawaiian Deep, a flexural trough); ledger C3-04 (trench, moat or trough) and C3-05 (never a ring). Our ETOPO 15" flood at -5,486 m (pool.py): one connected strip of about 9,200 km² (155.54-154.09 W, 19.05-21.20 N, deepest -5,764 m) along the NE base, continuing north past the grid edge. Drawn only from those cells; runs out of both frame edges; never cropped into a closed basin. "The last water" is true within the frame only. At this level the Big Island is joined to Maui by dry ridge (C3-08): Maui stays out of frame (bearing ~292°; the right frame edge is ~263-268°), and nothing says or shows the island standing alone.
- "How deep? About eighteen thousand feet." / ABOUT 18,000 FEET DEEP. Spine #4 ("about 5,500 m (18,000 ft) deep") and the brief's unit table; ledger C3-04 wording. Moat floor -5,350 to -5,600 m within 100 km. Pool surface at exactly -5,486 m; ABOUT stays on screen.
- "More than halfway up a mountain." Ledger C12-01 and C3-02, from the USGS FAQ: Mauna Kea is 13,796 ft above sea level and about 19,700 ft below, so sea level is 58.8% of the way up from its USGS base and 56.7% from the ~5,500 m moat (pool.py). Also holds for Mauna Loa: about 54% from its ~5 km submarine base (C3-02), about 76% from the dented base (spine #2). The payoff frame shows base, surfer and summit together. "Halfway" is a proportion, not a big number.
- "And he has no idea, this is what's under Hawaiʻi." True: from the sea surface the flank is hidden behind the shelf edge (line of sight on ETOPO).
- Gags (overflow, chain, plug, bathtub ring, "Sorry, buddy", waiting for a wave, "Fill it back up") are obviously playful; the description says "The plug is not real. The moat is."

Spoken numbers: 1 ("eighteen thousand"). On-screen numbers: 1 (18,000).

Not said (held for the long-form and Short 7): why the moat exists, Everest, 56,000 ft, 33,500 ft. "Five volcanoes become one" stays cut (C3-07).

**VERIFY (production checks, not narration)**
- Surfer photo: public domain only, anonymous, seen from behind; never Duke Kahanamoku or any named surfer; never an identifiable Native Hawaiian waterman as the stranded figure.
- On the HMRG 50 m bake, re-check the payoff lines of sight (ETOPO margins: surfer +26 m, Mauna Kea summit +90 m). Mauna Loa's summit at (246, 316) is hidden by 29 m on ETOPO: do not label it either way.
- Drowned reef steps only where the HMRG grid shows terraces (C12-10).
- Read the reef depth under the break from the NCEI DEM; it retimes the 3.35 s landing.
- Honoliʻi lies on Mauna Kea lavas (USGS geologic map, Sherrod et al. 2007). The line holds either way.
- Hawaiian pronunciations on wehewehe.org (lexicon open item 1).
- Licence: confirm the paid ElevenLabs plan with the owner before a monetised upload (covers the voice and music.py's music).

### 7. Renders: new for this Short vs reused

**New, in the Shorts project only (the long-form edit stays locked).**
1. **Camera path S1_SURF.** One continuous camera, 870 frames at 30 fps, 1080x1920 (1440x2560 master only after the 1440p encode test passes). TerrainStage at VE 1.0. Each segment eased on its destination key, one velocity peak per segment; sub-frame blur on any pan over 20 px/frame. Keys:
   - P0 (0.00, and 28.35-29.00 at rest): 4.0 m from the surfer on his seaward side (bearing 70°), 1.4 m above the 0 m water; heading 247, pitch -3, VFOV 58; surfer at the Honoliʻi break, ~100 m off the boulder beach at the stream mouth, placed on the NCEI 1/3" Hilo DEM (ETOPO-scale 19.762 N 155.091 W).
   - 0.40-3.35: the camera sinks 1.7 m at ~60% of the water's rate; pitch -3 to -10. 3.35-4.80: hold with a 1% push; pull-back starts at 4.80.
   - K1 6.00: 19.7865 N 155.0454 W, +250 m, heading 240, pitch -21, VFOV 54; surfer at (541, 334).
   - K2 8.00: 19.9010 N 154.8342 W, +250 m, heading 241, pitch -19.
   - K3 10.00: 19.9638 N 154.7181 W, +250 m, heading 243, pitch -19.
   - K4 12.30: 20.0220 N 154.6102 W, +250 m, heading 245, pitch -21.
   - K5 15.40 (moat pose): 20.0578 N 154.5437 W, +250 m, heading 247, pitch -30, VFOV 54; plug at (687, 1206); 1.5% push to 18.10.
   - K6 19.60 (payoff pose): same position, heading 250, pitch -20, VFOV 60; 1% push to 25.40. ETOPO-checked (pay.py, pool.py): Mauna Kea summit (683, 295); surfer (252, 372); shelf edge y 397; pool edge y 874-1313 across the frame; plug (582, 1482).
   - 25.40-28.35: fly-in back to P0, distance and height interpolated in log space (look book S14); heading 250 to 247, pitch -20 to -3, VFOV 60 to 58; arrives with zero velocity; the camera always stays above the rising water.
2. **Water level.** One flat plane with a foam rim at the contact line; no waterfalls, no outflow current, no wave front. Drain: 0 m (to 0.40), -0.6 (1.0), -1.6 (1.6), -2.4 (2.4), -2.8 (3.35, the board lands), -6 (4.5), -20 (5.0), -60 (6.0), -400 (7.0), -1,500 (8.0), -3,000 (10.0), -4,500 (12.0), -5,200 (13.6), -5,486 (15.41, ease-out, held to 25.375). Refill: -400 (26.60), -2.8 (27.60), -2.0 (27.75, the board lifts), 0 (28.35, ease-out, then held).
3. **Honoliʻi close tile.** NCEI 1/3" Hilo DEM (or USGS 3DEP 10 m) with an ortho drape; hand-placed basalt boulders within 150 m of P0 and the reef under the break; a small-swell surf shader using only periods L/3, /5, /7, /10, /15; the salt-white bathtub ring on the 0 m contour, revealed as the water leaves, never thinner than 6 px on screen. Needs the shared near-surface water pass (shorts_plan.md section 5B), since the eye is 1.4 m above the water.
4. **Props.** Chrome overflow plate (18 m wide, oval, two slots) on the boulder beach ~100 m beyond him. Chrome ball chain: from the plate down the boulders, along the reef 3 m to his left (passing ~3.7 m left of P0), then out along the 60° line to the plug; balls 1.0 m near the plate growing to ~60 m at the plug, never thinner than 8 px; states SLACK (one authored pose, used at frame 0 and 15.41-29.0, sway L/5) and TAUT (0.03-15.41, snaps on frame 1 with a spray line). Black rubber plug, 1.2 km across, in a chrome plughole ring at 20.0363 N 154.6118 W (ETOPO -5,579 m, under 93 m of pool water). Whirl shader over the plug (12.50-15.41) and a bubble dome at 15.41.
5. **Paper surfer.** Cut with cutout.py and card.py from a public-domain photo of an anonymous surfer seen from behind; warm off-white 3 px paper edge; the board a separate card; soft contact shadow; true scale, 36% of frame height at P0. Rig: ride bob (period L/10, phase-locked); follows the water while floating; landing squash and overshoot (3.35); yaw wobbles ±12° (3.95, 4.40); hop (20.80); 15° forward lean (21.40-21.85, held to 23.10); dry ride bob (23.10-25.40); lift back onto the wave (27.75).
6. **Loupe camera** (inset only, 20.05-24.85): a 600x600 render in a circle centred at (600, 840); camera 4.0 m behind him at bearing 70°, 1.4 m above the dry reef; heading 247, pitch -6, FOV 36; the surfer ~63% of the loupe (about 380 px).
7. **UI layer** (drawn after POST). Kicker and headline 0.00-3.30 and back 28.30-28.80. Depth gauge (T.gauge; in 0.40-0.90, out 27.0-27.6): top-left, bar x 58-78, y 236-516 (14.6% of frame height), 64 px chrome overflow icon at the top tick (0 m), bottom -6,000 m, turquoise below the marker, basalt above, 40 px marker, no digits. Label stack LEDGE / DROP-OFF / MOUNTAIN at caps 110/130/150. Number block. Ring with a straight leader to the loupe. Captions per the house spec. All text at x 120-849 and y ≥ 180; every cap ≥ 80 px (4.2%). Widths measured with fontTools on engine/www/fonts: Anton WATCH THE 526 / SURFER 345 at cap 110; LEDGE 281 at 110; DROP-OFF 533 at 130; MOUNTAIN 665 at 150; 18,000 725 at 245; widest caption card 611 at 80. Barlow Condensed 800: HAWAIʻI 330 at cap 80; FEET DEEP 602 at 110.
8. **Audio.** A 16-bar cue in the LF music palette (pizzicato bass, marimba, strings), from music.py (dur+3) or edited from the LF bed, conformed to 3840/L BPM, dips of -10 dB at 2.2-3.3 and 24.0-25.0, hits snapped to the nearest beat within 0.1 s. SFX: CLANK, chain rattle, THOOMP, gurgles, BOING (x2), paper flap (x2), label slaps (x3), PLUNK, bubble burp, glass tick, tub-filling gurgle-up (no roar), GLUG with a 0.25 s pre-lap. No motif more than 3 times. Mix -14 LUFS, true peak ≤ -1.0 dBTP, VO at least 12 LU above the music, comedic SFX in the gaps at about -16 dB.

**Reused as-is:** the ep02 Big Island terrain stack (HMRG 50 m into ETOPO 2022); the water-plane shader with foam rim and turquoise depth gradient; the Hawaiian Deep pool as ETOPO draws it (the LF Ch3 -4,000 to -5,500 m step); the LF refill curve shape, time-compressed for 25.375-26.60; the POST grade with the NO OCEAN exposure lift; Anton, Barlow Condensed 800, #FFD23F and T.gauge; the LF rumble, riser and gurgle SFX. No long-form frame or camera is reused.

**Look and QC gates.** Colours: dark basalt #2E2924 to #4A4038; moat mud #4E4C43; sparse cream reef; turquoise #22C3D6 (shallows) to #0F86A3 (deep). Tan ≤ 10% per frame (gate.py). Mean luma ≥ 85 per frame. Phone sheet at 390 px wide; a type check by hand (caps, 4-word headline, x ≤ 849). Loop gates as above. ASR on the VO, "Hawaii" first. A 10-15 s phone motion test of 0-6 s (waterline shimmer, chain spray, landing timing with sound). Review stills (frame 0, each beat, the last frame, the seam) go to the owner for an OK before the full render.

### 8. Title, description, link

**Title:** Hawaiʻi's Surfers Are Halfway Up a Mountain (43 characters)
- Proper noun first, plain-claim form. No clone words (drain/drained, hidden/hiding, bottom/beneath, nobody, found); no match for the niche report's banned-word regex.
- ytsearch probes 2026-11-08 ("surfers halfway up a mountain", "what's under hawaii", "hawaii surfers mountain underwater"): no Short or long-form uses this premise or these first 4 words; no top-20 title shares 3 consecutive words with it.
- It leaves the farmed plug-pulling family behind ("I Pulled the Plug on the Ocean" at 10M, 88K and the copies). True: sea level is 57-59% of the way up Mauna Kea from the moat or its base.
- Fallback (owner's choice): "What Hawaiʻi's Surfers Can't See" (32). Integrator note: kept as the fallback only, because Short 2's title also starts "You Can't..." the next day.

**Description (exact):**

> Pull the plug on the ocean beside Hawaiʻi's Big Island and a surf break off Hilo turns into a ledge, a drop-off, then a mountain, with the last water in a moat at its foot, about 18,000 feet down.
> Why is there a moat at the foot of Hawaiʻi? Full film: Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down) [long-form link]
> Real seafloor and coast data at true vertical scale: University of Hawaiʻi SOEST multibeam synthesis, NOAA ETOPO 2022 and the NOAA NCEI Hilo coastal elevation model. Surfer: [public-domain photo credit]. The plug is not real. The moat is.
> #Hawaii #BigIsland #Hilo #surfing #geography

The long-form title is quoted exactly as published, with plain "Hawaii".

**Tags:** Hawaii, Big Island, Hilo, Mauna Kea, Hawaii without water, ocean drained, Hawaiian Deep, surfing Hawaii, geography.

**Link setup** (every step needs the owner's explicit OK; we publish, schedule and change nothing ourselves): on D0 the long-form goes live first; this Short follows 10-30 minutes later, in the same hour. In YouTube Studio set this Short's Related Video to the long-form, and paste the long-form URL into line 2 of the description. The Short shows the moat but never explains it; the long-form's payoff (the weight denting the sea floor) answers line 2, so the Related Video is the natural next tap.

**Pinned comment** (after the owner's OK): "Have you ever paddled out off Hilo? Would you still, knowing you're more than halfway up a mountain?"

**Before upload:** run the whole-word, case-insensitive AI-term grep on the title, description, tags, pinned comment, SRT, burned-in captions and narration. Expect 0 hits ("ai" inside Hawaii is not a hit).

Panel score on the draft: 66. All 11 must-fixes are in this version: frame-1 headline (kicker HAWAIʻI + WATCH THE SURFER, back in by 28.80); "cliff" replaced by "drop-off" (C12-10); retitled away from the plug clone family; readable hook subject (surfer 36% of frame height, plate 29% of width, chain balls ~145 px in the left half, visible drop by 1.6 s); timeline built from the real take ("Now look back up."); the 22.45-26.2 dead hold killed and the loupe at 600 px; labels held ≥ 2 s; all text at x ≤ 849; ASCII "Hawaii" in TTS with ASR check; the Hilo tsunami read defused (vertical level drop, tub-filling refill, anonymous back-turned surfer); description wording "a moat at its foot" (C3-05).

## Short 2 (P2-02-walk-to-lanai + S02-three-islands-become-one): "You Can't Walk From Maui to Lānaʻi (Unless...)"

| | |
|---|---|
| Release | **D+1**, same clock hour as the long-form (slot 2 of 7) |
| Length | 25.000 s (750 frames); 11 bars / 27.5 s if the real take does not fit |
| The one big number | none (zero numbers on screen or spoken) |
| Voice | Rodney - Clear Educator, `eY2VJs4Gi8QKG2RV01sS`, `eleven_v4`, passed explicitly |
| Format | 1080x1920, 30 fps; music 96 BPM, 10 bars, built to loop |
| Related Video | "Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down)" |

Times are estimates calibrated on Rodney's measured take (audio/rodney_test/timing.json). Every picture event is tied to the quoted word and moves with it once the real take is aligned.

### Integrator changes (2026-11-08)

1. **The bath plug and chain became a chest freezer.** "Unless you pull the plug." became **"Unless you put some ocean in the freezer."**, and "The Ice Age pulled this plug for real." became **"The Ice Age did this for real."** Reasons:
   - Short 1, released the day before, is built on a bathtub plug and chain at a shoreline with a paper figure. Two plug Shorts on consecutive days break the playbook's distinctness rule (no shared opening shot or device) and would look templated.
   - "Pull the plug on the ocean" is the farmed clone premise (23M, 10M, 214K and at least 8 copies). Speaking it in the hook put us inside that family.
   - The freezer is the honest cartoon of the real mechanism: in an ice age, water leaves the ocean and is stored as ice on land, and the sea falls (C12-04). "The Ice Age did this for real" is now literal, VERIFY (g) on the figurative plug wording is closed, and the loop rhymes ("the ice melted" into "the freezer").
   - Every beat, gag, camera and loop mechanic of the writer's version is kept; only the prop, two lines and the SFX change. Times from 3.4 s to 20.5 s moved by +0.15 to +0.55 s and three VO holes shrank so the seam stays at 25.0 s.
   - If the owner prefers the plug version, it can be restored by swapping these lines and props back, and Short 1 would then need a different device.
2. **TTS text uses the lexicon respellings** (lah-NAH-ee, moh-loh-KAH-ee) instead of an alias dictionary, the series method (lexicon.json: no U+02BB or kahakō in TTS text until a test render passes). Maui is sent plain; never "MOW-ee" (lexicon).
3. **All text at x ≤ 849** (channel rule): the LĀNAʻI slip moved to x 430-840, the Shot B MAUI slip to x 560-840, the IT HAPPENED stamp to x 110-845.
4. **Burned-in captions added** in the house style (shorts_plan.md section 4), suppressed while the NOBODY / WALKED IT slip shows the same words. Our own SRT with native spellings is also uploaded.
5. **ʻOkina glyph:** the LĀNAʻI and MOLOKAʻI slips are Barlow Condensed 800, whose U+02BB draws as a backtick. type.js draws Barlow's U+2018 glyph for U+02BB (house rule); the text stays U+02BB.
6. Lānaʻi is spoken 3 times per loop (the draft said 4).

### 1. Narration and TTS

**Text sent to TTS** (one request, rotated so the loop seam is one natural take). Voice "Rodney - Clear Educator" (`eY2VJs4Gi8QKG2RV01sS`), model `eleven_v4`, the locked long-form settings (`"settings": {}`). Generate 3 takes (`"take": 1, 2, 3`) and pick one by the listen test below.

> Then the ice melted, the sea crept back, and ever since, you can't walk to lah-NAH-ee. Unless you put some ocean in the freezer. Lah-NAH-ee snaps onto Maui. Moh-loh-KAH-ee snaps onto lah-NAH-ee. Sorry, ferry. You're grounded. The Ice Age did this for real. The catch? Nobody ever walked it. People hadn't made it here yet.

As heard and captioned (caption_map spellings): "You can't walk to Lānaʻi. Unless you put some ocean in the freezer. Lānaʻi snaps onto Maui. Molokaʻi snaps onto Lānaʻi. Sorry, ferry. You're grounded. The Ice Age did this for real. The catch? Nobody ever walked it. People hadn't made it here yet. Then the ice melted, the sea crept back, and ever since,"

55 words, all written new for this Short. It reuses none of the long-form's lines ("Drop the sea four hundred feet", "They become one island", "That's not a what-if", "No ferry needed"). Estimated speech about 20.3 s (about 0.19 s per syllable mid-sentence, 0.5-0.85 s on sentence-final words, comma pauses 0.08-0.15 s).

**How the take is placed (build to the real take)**
1. Align the chosen take for word timings. Cut it at sentence ends, and inside the comma pause after "since,".
2. Seam: "you can't walk to Lānaʻi." starts at 0.05. "...ever since," ends so that the take's own comma pause spans the seam, plus at most 0.1 s of room tone: about 0.16 s at the end and 0.05 s at the start.
3. Lines are placed only by adding or trimming silence between sentences. No time-stretching.
4. Planned placement (estimates; every picture event is anchored to a word):

| Line | Planned time |
|---|---|
| VO1 "You can't walk to Lānaʻi." | 0.05-1.66 |
| VO2 "Unless you put some ocean in the freezer." ("Unless" before 2.2 s) | 1.86-4.05 |
| VO3 "Lānaʻi snaps onto Maui." | 4.95-6.79 |
| VO4 "Molokaʻi snaps onto Lānaʻi." | 6.99-9.17 |
| VO5 "Sorry, ferry." then "You're grounded." | 10.07-11.50, 12.05-13.03 |
| VO6 "The Ice Age did this for real." | 13.33-15.26 |
| VO7 "The catch? Nobody ever walked it." | 15.61-18.24 |
| VO8 "People hadn't made it here yet." | 18.54-20.47 |
| VO9 "Then the ice melted, the sea crept back, and ever since," | 21.28-24.84 |

   Holes between lines: 0.20, 0.90 (the ocean pours into the freezer), 0.20, 0.90 (rim and crash zoom), 0.55 (sad toot), 0.30, 0.35, 0.30, 0.81 (the comic awkward beat). None is over 1.2 s.
5. If the real take runs long: shrink the 0.90, 0.90 and 0.81 holes to 0.8, 0.8 and 0.6. If speech plus the minimum holes still exceeds about 24.8 s, go to 11 bars (27.5 s) and give the extra time to those three holes (at most 1.2 s each) and to G1 (+0.5 s). If the take runs short, grow those three holes up to 1.2 s.
6. Choosing the take (phone speaker): every Hawaiian name right; "since," keeps a continuing comma tone; played cold from 0.00, "You can't walk to Lānaʻi." sounds like an opening line, not half a sentence.

**Pronunciation** (lexicon.json; check every entry on wehewehe.org, and with a Hawaiian speaker if possible, before the render)
- Spoken by Rodney:
  - Lānaʻi (3 times) → TTS "lah-NAH-ee" (lexicon alt "laah-NAH-ee"). Hawaiian [laːˈnɐʔi]: long first "ah", stress on NAH, a light glottal catch before the final "ee". Never "luh-NYE".
  - Molokaʻi → TTS "moh-loh-KAH-ee" ([ˌmoloˈkɐʔi]). Four syllables; never "MOLLY-kai" or "moh-loh-KYE".
  - Maui → plain "Maui" (English voices say it right), target /ˈmaʊ.i/; fall back to "MAO-ee" only if the listen test fails. Never "MOW".
- On screen only: LĀNAʻI, MAUI, MOLOKAʻI (as above).
- Description, pinned comment and tags only: Hawaiʻi "hah-WHY-ee" ([həˈwɐiʔi]); Kahoʻolawe "kah-hoh-oh-LAH-veh"; Maui Nui "MAO-ee NOO-ee" ("Big Maui"); ʻAuʻau "ao-AO" ([ʔɐuˈʔɐu]; the Grigg paper title prints "Au'au").
- Production place names, never spoken (rule-derived; add to the lexicon and check on wehewehe.org): Launiupoko "lao-nee-oo-POH-koh"; Lahaina "lah-HIGH-nah"; Olowalu "oh-loh-WAH-loo"; Lānaʻihale "lah-NAH-ee-HAH-leh"; Kalohi "kah-LOH-hee"; Pailolo "pie-LOH-loh".
- ASR-check the take (small.en, medium.en) through caption_map: "Lānaʻi", "Molokaʻi", "Maui" and "freezer" must come back as scripted.

### 2. Second-by-second table

Shared rules for every shot: one sun (azimuth ~110°, elevation ~20°, warm low light); turquoise water with depth gradient and foam rims; exposed seabed in real tones (dark wet basalt near the old coasts, slate-olive carbonate on the shelves, dark reef pinnacles); turquoise pools only in the real enclosed basins; tan ≤ 10% of the frame (gate.py); no gull sounds (gulls are rare visitors in Hawaiʻi). Text: everything inside x 72-849, y 200-1500, except captions (house spec). Paper slips: dark ink #14202A on off-white #F6F1E4, tilted 2-4°, soft shadow. No black boxes, arrows, dashed lines, odometer or number anywhere.

**0.0-1.0 · SHOT A = FRAME 0**
- Picture:
  - NEW shore-level vertical camera. ONE continuous render from t = -2.2 to 5.05; its first 2.2 s become 22.80-25.00.
  - Location: Launiupoko, West Maui, about 20.842N 156.650W, chosen over Olowalu (site of the 1790 massacre). VERIFY it is outside the 2023 burn area. No structures in frame.
  - Camera 2.2 m up, 9 m behind the tourist, heading 258.5° (Lānaʻihale at bearing 261°, 23 km), pitch -7°, vFOV 32°, pushing in 2% per 2.7 s.
  - Horizon at about y 550. Lānaʻi's ridge across the whole horizon, peak near x 700, y 405. Lahaina (bearing 323°), Molokaʻi and Kahoʻolawe out of frame.
  - The paper tourist (the long-form 0:23.6 cut-out, red aloha shirt) at the waterline: feet at x 470, y 1350, head at y 700 (34% of frame height), three-quarter view facing frame-left, suitcase in his left hand.
  - THE FREEZER: a white chest freezer (paper cut-out of a real photo) stands in the swash just left of him, x 120-420, y 1080-1340. Its lid card is hinged at the back and propped 25° open by his right hand; a curl of frost spills over the lip (loop-clock sprite). He leans in 12°, peering into it. At 390 px it reads as a man with a suitcase holding open a freezer in the surf.
  - A swash covers his front foot and the freezer's base (peak 0.15).
  - A paper ferry bobs 400 m out (x 560-760, y 515-600; 2.5 s bob).
- Text: LĀNAʻI paper slip (Barlow Condensed 800 cap 90, small Myth Juice stamp in the corner, tilted -3°) pinned above the peak at x 430-840, y 230-360. MAUI slip (cap 90) at x 520-780, y 1330-1450 beside his feet. Captions from 0.05: YOU CAN'T / WALK TO / LĀNAʻI.
- VO: "You can't walk to Lānaʻi." (0.05-1.66; "Lānaʻi" at 0.81)
- Sound: surf bed (loop). Bar 1: a caper theme of pizzicato strings, upright bass and a brushed tick (not a ukulele travel bed). Swash hiss peaks at 0.15.

**1.0-2.0**
- Picture: 1.00 the compressor kicks on: the cabinet shudders, its lid light flicks on, a frost puff rolls out. 1.20 THE ANOMALY: the backwash keeps going, too far and too fast, and a thin rope of seawater peels off the swash, curls up the freezer's side and pours over the lip into it. 1.86 he plants his feet and braces the lid wider.
- Text: LĀNAʻI + MAUI; caption UNLESS from 1.86.
- VO: "...Lānaʻi." ends at 1.66; "Unless..." starts at 1.86.
- Sound: compressor THUNK and hum (1.00) with a bass "boing". A slurp enters low at 1.20, clearly audible by 1.30. One sour bass note at 1.25.

**2.0-3.0**
- Picture: 2.40 on "put": he flings the lid fully open; it bangs against its hinge stop with a frost blast. 2.95 on "ocean": the rope swells into a thick twisting arc of seawater from the receding sea up into the freezer. The sea starts falling fast.
- Text: LĀNAʻI + MAUI; captions YOU PUT / SOME OCEAN.
- VO: "...you put some ocean..." ("put" at 2.40, "ocean" at 2.95)
- Sound: bar 2 at 2.5. Lid bang at 2.40; a big rushing slurp from 2.95 (a drain glug run backwards, not surf). The music low-pass sweep starts.

**3.0-4.05**
- Picture: 3.55 on "freezer": the arc becomes a torrent; the freezer frosts white and its compressor roars. 3.60-4.20 the LĀNAʻI slip unpins and is sucked along the arc into the freezer. 4.00 the waterline races out across the reef flat, exposing wet black basalt boulders, dark sand, slate-olive flats and turquoise tide pools.
- Text: MAUI holds (LĀNAʻI gone by 4.20); captions IN THE / FREEZER.
- VO: "...in the freezer." (to 4.05)
- Sound: paper-flap MOTIF #1 (3.60-4.20), the series sound mark. Rushing water. Riser starts.

**4.05-5.05**
- Picture: 4.15 paper double-take (a 0.15 s squash): he looks at the freezer, then at the camera. 4.40 the ferry grounds 400 m out and tilts; at 4.50 it flops onto its side. 4.55-5.05 an eased log-altitude lift (0.5 s, sub-frame motion blur): the beach, the freezer and the MAUI slip drop away. 5.05 a hidden cut inside the blur to SHOT B.
- Text: MAUI gone by 4.85; no caption (VO hole 4.05-4.95).
- Sound: wooden clonk and creak at 4.50. Whoosh up at 4.55. The riser climbs.

- Picture: 24.06 his last hop lands him on his mark, with a thunk and a sand puff. 24.20 he grabs the freezer lid. 24.375 he swings it down to its frame-0 prop angle (25°) with a soft clunk; the frost curl returns to its frame-0 size; the compressor is off (it kicks on again at 1.00). 24.45-24.80 he settles into the frame-0 pose: leaning in 12°, right hand on the lid, suitcase in his left hand. Cloud shadows are back to normal speed by 24.60. 24.85-25.00 a swash runs up the sand and touches his front foot and the freezer's base exactly at 25.00, which is frame 0.
