### 3. Hook

- 0.03 s VO: "This is the most optimistic sign in Hawaiʻi." (8 words, 13 syllables, ends 2.80). A flat statement about an absurd prop: no question and no menu.
- **Frame 0 is the thumbnail, already moving:** a cream postcard-paper board reading NEW ISLAND / COMING / SOON (navy Anton, caps 116/96/96, upper third, x 232-828, y 236-608), nailed to a yellow/navy striped survey pole rising out of open, deep-blue ocean about 19 miles off Kīlauea's coast, with a broken foam collar at the waterline; the Big Island's long low silhouette on the horizon; nothing else in frame. The hook phrase is the biggest line. Sound off, the 4-word sign asks the question itself: what new island, out here?
- **Measured on the real engine frame** (near-surface ocean pass, board composited at final size): mean luma 146; lower half 98.7% blue-dominant; pale-grey pixels 1.8% (against 33% in the old previz that read as a beach); tan 0.5%; near-black 0.4%.
- **Motion inside the hook:** 0.90 a swell slaps the pole and spray hits the board; 1.80, under SIGN IN HAWAIʻI., the sea starts sliding away beneath the sign, the foam collar drops out of frame and the wet striped pole streams out of the water; 3.00 "Drain the ocean." with the gurgle; 3.95 the camera dives after the waterline. The thumb sees the spectacle begin before 2 s.

### 4. Payoff

Three stacked payoffs, each new information, the last closest to the loop.
1. **Depth** (the strongest stretch). The camera rides the falling waterline down the striped pole, through a converging drain swirl and joint clunks speeding into a rattle, with Rodney's deadpan "Keep going." The odometer crawls 3,180... 3,190 and lands 3,200 FEET on "down". THUNK, the music cuts dead: black pillow lava at the pole's foot. "A volcano." One crane and pull-back shows the summit as a tiny dark crescent islet in turquoise shallows under the giant Big Island massif: "The youngest in the whole Hawaiian chain."
2. **New twist** (true, unspent). Low beside the islet a paper tag swings in on the pole reading LŌʻIHI and flips over to KAMAʻEHUAKANALOA: "Not even born, and already renamed." Then "Back up." completes the narrator's command motif (Drain the ocean / Follow the pole / Keep going / Back up), and the camera rockets 3,400 ft up the pole to the sign, now hanging over the lowered sea.
3. **Time answer and sticker button.** "Grand opening?" (a 0.55 s guess beat under the tilt-up) "In thousands of generations." (the USGS's own phrase; new wording against the long-form's "thousands of years from now") over a time-lapse: clouds race, the sea surges back and splashes the board; generations pass and it is still just a sign in the sea. Deadpan "Maybe." A yellow -ISH sticker slaps on: COMING SOON-ISH. A gust peels it off (a second micro-gag that also resets the frame), and "Which means..." hands back to the opening claim.

### 5. Loop check

**Last line into first line:** "...Grand opening? In thousands of generations. Maybe. Which means... this is the most optimistic sign in Hawaiʻi. Drain the ocean. Follow the pole..." One natural sentence: "Which means..." (open, rising) completes into the opening statement; a first-time viewer hears a clean claim.

**The seam gap is a real gap, not a guess:** generate "Which means... this is the most optimistic sign in Hawaii.", measure G, place s13 so that tail silence + 0.03 s head offset = G (s13 ends at 25.00 - (G - 0.03)), cut at a zero crossing with 5-8 ms fades; the opener check is done by a person.

**Last frame against first frame**
- Same camera at both ends: K0 (5 m from the pole on bearing 157.13°, eye 0.6 m, heading 343°, pitch -3°, vfov 30°), static from 23.3 (the sticker push returns exactly).
- State by 24.50: water at 0 since 19.80; spray gone by 20.20; no wet shading (the pole shader has no wetness term and the splash is particles only); sticker out of frame by 23.70; gust shake at 0 by 24.4; sway 2.5 s period with 10 whole cycles (the rocket wobble decays to 0 by 19.5; the time-lapse adds exactly 4 extra cycles); clear sky at both ends.
- Captions switch hard by design (WHICH MEANS... until 25.00, THIS IS THE from 0.03); seam metrics are measured on the picture layer before captions.
- **Measured on real engine frames**, after making every time-driven water term periodic (waves at periods 25/n s; micro chop, foam collar, swirl and large-scale slicks driven by cos/sin(2πt/25)), grain off: frames 0 vs 749 (first vs last) MAE 1.91/255; 749 vs 750 (an ordinary frame step) 1.91; 0 vs 750 (one full period) 0.002. The seam step equals an ordinary step: an invisible join. With grain on it was 2.66 against a 2.34 ordinary step. Gate ≤ 5.
- Found and fixed: the stock HawaiiStage water drifts linearly in time (fbm offsets time*0.004 and time*0.03), which breaks any loop (1.43-1.88 MAE over one period before the fix). This fix is shared with every looping Short (shorts_plan.md section 5B).

**Sound:** the wave bed is one seamless 25.0 s loop, crossfaded tail into head. Music 10 bars at 96 bpm; bar 10 resolves into bar 1. One slap+creak straddles the cut (attack at 24.92, decay through 0.35); no second slap, so no stutter. Splash and gust tails ducked -10 dB under "Which means".

**QC gate on the final render:** first/last MAE ≤ about 5/255 on the full composite with the board; concatenate the Short with itself and run temporal_scan.py (no "cut" or "flash"); audio_scan.py: no click at the seam.

### 6. Facts

1. **"Three thousand two hundred feet down"** and the counter 3,200 FEET. Spine #16 (summit 975 m / 3,200 ft below sea level). USGS HVO Volcano Watch, 27 Jan 2022 (the spine #16 URL), re-fetched 2026-11-08: "rising to 3,189 ft (975 m) below sea level". Brief unit table: -975 m = 3,200 ft. With the summit correction the counter shows the true waterline depth in feet, rounded to 10, at every frame, and lands 3,200 at -975 m (3,199 ft). The ETOPO grid smooths the summit to -1,023/-1,026 m.
2. **"A volcano."** Spine #16.
3. **"The youngest in the whole Hawaiian chain."** Spine #16. USGS, same page: "the youngest volcano in the long line of the Hawaiian-Emperor chain".
4. **"Not even born, and already renamed."** Spine #16 (renamed 2021). USGS, same page: "in July 2021, Lōʻihi Seamount was renamed Kamaʻehuakanaloa by the Hawaii Board on Geographic Names"; the name Lōʻihi dates from 1955. "Not even born" is a metaphor for not yet emerged as an island (spine #16: "it will be millennia before" it emerges). "already renamed" does not claim how many times.
5. **"Grand opening? In thousands of generations. Maybe."** VERIFY closed by the writer on 2026-11-08 on the spine #16 URL: "In thousands of generations, perhaps our descendants will witness Kamaʻehuakanaloa ... finally emerging and forming a new island." (from a fetched page summary; recheck the exact wording on the page before quoting it anywhere). "Maybe" = USGS's "perhaps"; "generations" is USGS's own word; consistent with spine #16 "millennia" and the brief's "thousands of years at least". Add it to the ep02 ledger before lock.
6. **Sign NEW ISLAND / COMING SOON.** A joke prop, hedged in the narration ("Maybe.") and on the sign itself (the -ISH sticker). Spine #16: say it "could" become the next island; the description says "could one day rise above the sea as a new Hawaiian island".
7. **Tag LŌʻIHI to KAMAʻEHUAKANALOA.** Spine #16 (formerly Lōʻihi, renamed 2021). Screen only, never spoken.
8. **"This is the most optimistic sign in Hawaiʻi."** A joke about our own prop; no factual claim.
9. **"Drain the ocean." / "Back up."** Imperatives that frame the drain as a thought experiment; no claim. The description says "lowering the sea is a thought experiment".
10. **Visuals.** At about -1,050 m (corrected grid) the summit shows as a small crescent islet; nothing is said or shown about its shape as a future island (never "this is what the island will look like"). The same DEM has a bench about 24 km north at about -1,000 m that also surfaces: re-run sill.py and the flood-fill on the production grid. VE 1.0 in every shot.
11. **Description lines.** "About 19 miles off Kīlauea's coast", "youngest volcano in the Hawaiian-Emperor chain", "millennia", renamed July 2021 and "the reddish child of Kanaloa" are all from the same USGS page; the description writes "perhaps thousands of generations" as a paraphrase, not a quote.

Never said: erupting, active, 1996, earthquake, Everest, 56,000 ft, dent. One big number (3,200). No on-screen sources. Open VERIFY items: none in narration or on screen once item 5 is in the ledger.

### 7. Renders: new for this Short vs reused

**Proven in the writer's fork** (scratchpad; the repo engine is untouched): /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-juicytea-media/4a181100-5b6b-5601-bf64-5ea13c092764/scratchpad/short06v2/ with www/s06/hterrain_s06.js (a fork of engine/www/lab/hawaii/hterrain.js; patch.py plus inline edits), www/s06.html (the proof page; render with `node engine/still.cjs --www <that www> --page s06.html --w 1080 --h 1920`), out/*/f*.jpg (frames), mock_v2.py (compositor), m_phone390.jpg (6 key frames at phone width), m_sheet_guides.jpg (safe-zone guides), m_f0.jpg, m_desc.jpg, m_wide.jpg, m_tag.jpg, m_grand.jpg, m_payoff.jpg.
1. **Near-surface ocean pass** (built and measured). Root cause of the beach-like frame 0: HawaiiStage's camera near plane is 0.5 km, so all water within 500 m was clipped and the seabed 1 km down showed through; the skirt water grid's 4.4 km cells also mis-place the curvature drop by up to 0.38 m (matters at a 0.6 m eye). Fix A, split-depth render: a far pass (near plane 1.2 km), clear depth, then a near pass (0.2 m to 1.2 km), sky off. Fix B: a 3x3 km near water patch at 2.5 m cells, re-centred under the camera each frame (the water vertex shader takes lon/lat from the world position, so both water meshes share one material). Fix C: metre-scale waves, 12 directional deep-water components with periods 25/n s (n = 5-58, wavelengths about 0.25-39 m), faded by pixel footprint, plus periodic micro chop and turquoise crests. Fix D: a broken foam collar at the pole and log-spiral drain-swirl foam streaks. Fix E: every time-driven water term on a circle (cos/sin 2πt/L). Fix F: the scene origin at the pole for float precision. Results: frame 0 lower half 98.7% blue, 1.8% pale grey (was about 33%), mean luma 146; descent (desc2) and K0 over the lowered sea (vert2) show no exposed seabed.
2. **The 3D pole:** 975 m plus 1.45 m above sea level, 2.5 cm radius, 0.2 m yellow/navy bands, joint collars every 48.77 m below water only; 4096 height segments (one segment made the band edges jagged).
3. **Dark-basalt grade on all exposed rock:** basalt 0.017/0.017/0.020, sediment 0.027/0.025/0.024 (linear); key frames measure pale grey 1.0-1.8% and tan 0.4-1.2%.

**Still to build**
1. Port 1-2 into engine/www/lab/hawaii/hterrain.js as default-off options (split, nearPlane, the near patch, the near-wave uniforms, the pole, periodic drift), with the long-form safeguards in shorts_plan.md section 9.
2. Pillow-lava patch at the pole's foot for the THUNK and K_foot (the 100 m DEM alone gives a flat dark plane, test thunk2): about 40 m radius of instanced lumpy ellipsoids 0.5-1.5 m across, dark basalt with glassy rinds, wet sheen, run-off sheets; built in the engine, no generators.
3. Summit correction: a smooth bump (Gaussian sigma about 1.5 km, peak +48 m) on the summit so the grid maximum equals the USGS 975 m; then pick the islet hold level (about -1,050 m) and gate it with sill.py and the flood-fill. If the owner prefers raw data, key the counter to the grid summit instead.
4. Props: the board (series postcard paper #F6F1E4, text baked as a 4K texture, nailed to the pole, sway driven by a phase function); the -ISH sticker (yellow paper, slap and peel); the two-sided name tag (LŌʻIHI / KAMAʻEHUAKANALOA, flip, outside eyelet, string); spray particles (gone 0.4 s after each splash); time-lapse sky clouds (clear to clear); in wide shots the pole drawn at least 8 px wide in screen space (the proof cheated with 40 m and 4.5 m radii in wide2 and klow2).
5. One continuous camera spline: K0 → K_ride → THUNK → K_foot → crane/pull-back to K_wide → swoop to K_low → rocket to K_top (6-8 sub-frames of blur) → tilt to K0; cyclic at the ends; eased on the destination keys.
6. UI layer: counter (T.slot, Anton cap 240); captions (house spec); load both latin and latin-ext subsets (ʻ U+02BB in latin, Ō U+014C only in latin-ext; checked with fontTools).
7. Render: render_chunks.py --w 1080 --h 1920 into the Short's own chunk directory; then the loop-seam QC.

**Reused as-is:** HawaiiStage and its baked data (ETOPO 2022 15", Terrain Tiles, MODIS colour, AO); the 'day' look and the 'turq' water; the long-form's live drain mechanic; the Ch6 young-cone location and the COMING SOON idea; the Anton/Barlow type system and the T.slot odometer; library cues thunk_heavy, tick_counter, whoosh, the paper-flap motif, gurgle, riser.

**Clone-look check:** no caption box, no curved arrows, no dashed sea-level line (the sign marks sea level); dark basalt and turquoise/deep blue, never a tan basin; no "taller than Everest".

**Probe** (ytsearch, 2026-11-08): "hawaii most optimistic sign" returns zodiac Shorts only; "loihi renamed kamaehuakanaloa": best Short 9 views; "next Hawaiian island" Shorts sit at 1.5-2.3K. The premise is unclaimed but not proven to pull views.

### 8. Title, description, link

**Title:** Hawaiʻi's Most Optimistic Sign (30 characters; the proper noun first; the clone-word regex finds no match; whole-word AI check over title, description, narration, captions and on-screen strings: 0 hits).

**Description (paste-ready):**

> This sign hangs on a pole about 3,200 feet long. At its foot is the summit of Kamaʻehuakanaloa, the youngest volcano in the Hawaiian-Emperor chain, about 19 miles off Kīlauea's coast on Hawaiʻi Island.
>
> It could one day rise above the sea as a new Hawaiian island, but not soon: USGS scientists say it will take millennia, perhaps thousands of generations.
>
> Until July 2021 it was called Lōʻihi Seamount. The Hawaiʻi Board on Geographic Names renamed it Kamaʻehuakanaloa, "the reddish child of Kanaloa."
>
> How much of the Big Island is under the waves? The full film: Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down)
>
> The sign is a joke, and lowering the sea is a thought experiment. The depth is real: the summit lies about 3,200 feet (975 m) below sea level.
> Source: USGS Hawaiian Volcano Observatory, Volcano Watch (January 27, 2022). Seafloor and terrain: NOAA NCEI ETOPO 2022; Terrain Tiles (Mapzen and its data sources). Imagery: NASA GIBS (MODIS).
>
> #Hawaii #volcano #geography

**Link:** in YouTube Studio the owner sets the Short's Related video to "Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down)" (only with his OK; nothing is uploaded or changed by an agent). This Short feeds the film's Ch6 newborn-volcano beat without spending the film's payoff (no 56,000 ft, no dent, no Everest); its time line ("In thousands of generations. Maybe.") differs from the film's ("thousands of years from now").

**Pinned comment** (integrator addition; owner's OK): "Should the sign say COMING SOON, or COMING SOON-ISH? Be honest."

**Before upload:** re-run the whole-word check (AI|A.I.|artificial|synthetic|generated|generative|TTS|ElevenLabs...) and the clone-title regex on the final strings; confirm the related video is live; never credit or name the voice or music tool.

**For the 7-Shorts table:** hook type: absurd prop plus a superlative statement; opening shot: a sea-level close-up of a paper sign on a striped pole in open ocean; payoff device: rename-tag flip, then a time answer, then the -ISH sticker; loop line: "Which means... / this is the most optimistic sign in Hawaiʻi."

**Playbook lessons for the parent to record:** stock HawaiiStage cannot render water within 500 m of the camera (near plane 0.5 km), so near-surface shots need the split pass; every time-driven shader term in a looping Short must be periodic (cos/sin 2πt/L): measured one-period MAE dropped from 1.43-1.88 to 0.002.

## Short 5 (S10-midways-ships-arent-at-midway + P2-07-midway-carriers): "The Carriers Sunk at Midway Aren't at Midway"

| | |
|---|---|
| Release | **D+9**, same clock hour as the long-form (slot 6 of 7; moved from D+7, see below) |
| Length | T = 27.000 s nominal (810 frames, 0-809); lock T to the take |
| The one big number | MORE THAN 3 MILES DOWN |
| Voice | Rodney - Clear Educator, `eY2VJs4Gi8QKG2RV01sS`, `eleven_v4`, passed explicitly |
| Format | 1080x1920, 30 fps; VE 1.0 in every shot; solemn: no music, a drone bed only |
| Related Video | "Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down)" |

All picture events lock to the take's word timestamps; the times below are nominal.

### Integrator changes (2026-11-08)

1. **"Two have never been found." became "Two more are down there somewhere."** Ledger C5-13 bans "still lost" and "never found" for Sōryū and Hiryū (no public report of a discovery is not proof that they have not been found). The new line is true under every scenario: both carriers sank in June 1942, so their wrecks lie somewhere on the sea floor, and no position is drawn. The on-screen NEVER / FOUND became **SOMEWHERE**; the two discs still lose their ink and the third ping still gets no echo. The description line changed the same way. Hold after the line: 0.85 s (was 0.9) so T stays 27.000 s.
2. **Release moved to D+9** (from D+7). Short 4 (D+5) also opens on Midway's reef with a postcard; 4 days apart instead of 2. Short 6 takes D+7.
3. Captions follow the house spec (shorts_plan.md section 4): Anton cap 80, centred x 460 / y 1515 (was cap 77 at cap top 1452).
4. Pronunciation aligned with lexicon.json: Akagi "ah-KAH-ghee" (not AH-kah-gee); Papahānaumokuākea "pah-pah-HAH-now-moh-koo-ah-KEH-ah"; Hawaiʻi "hah-WHY-ee" (not hah-VAI-ee).
5. TTS settings: the locked long-form settings are the base; the solemn read (stability 0.6, style 0) is an A/B the owner picks by ear.

### 1. Narration and TTS

**Exact TTS input.** One ElevenLabs call (tts.py, one segment) with voice "Rodney - Clear Educator" (`eY2VJs4Gi8QKG2RV01sS`), model `eleven_v4`, passed explicitly. Base settings: the locked long-form settings; A/B: stability about 0.6, style 0, speed 1.0 (solemn).

> In June of nineteen forty-two, four Japanese carriers and the USS Yorktown went down. Drain the Pacific, and there's one, as far from Midway as Boston is from New York. More than three miles down, it's still sitting upright. Two more are down there somewhere. The carriers never even saw each other. They fought with planes, from far out at sea. That's why the carriers sunk at Midway aren't at Midway. In June of nineteen forty-two, four Japanese carriers and the USS Yorktown went down.

The repeated last sentence is a prosody guard and is never used: it puts the hook sentence in the middle of the paragraph, so Rodney does not read it with a falling, final close.

**Variants** (generate all three, choose on a phone speaker): V1 the text above; V2 the same with "...from far out at sea — that's why the carriers...", so "sea" keeps a continuing intonation; V3 V1 with "U.S.S." in place of "USS". Keep the take that passes all four checks: (a) piece B works cold (energy, an audible "the"); (b) "sea" does not fall to a final close; (c) the ship prefix is read "you-ess-ess" with no pause; (d) "one" in "there's one" is clear.

**How the take is cut (this makes the loop)**
- Find the natural gap (80-150 ms) between "why" and "the"; the cut point c is a zero crossing in the middle of that gap.
- Piece B runs from c to the end of "...aren't at Midway." plus 60 ms of room tone; it plays at 0.00, so "the" starts at about 0.04.
- Piece A runs from "In June" to c; it plays from 3.15.
- A ends at c and B starts at c, so across the seam the replay is the take's own continuous audio, with no fade and no splice.
- Inside A, change only the room-tone pauses: 0.5 s after "New York", 0.85 s after "somewhere", 0.3 s after "sea".
- T = 3.15 + the length of A; snap T to a whole frame by trimming the 0.15-0.25 s of room tone after the hook. Nominal T 27.000 s (810 frames).

**As heard, in order** (70 words; about 23.8 s of speech and 3.2 s of pauses):

| Time | Line |
|---|---|
| 0.04-2.95 | The carriers sunk at Midway aren't at Midway. |
| 3.15-9.00 | In June of nineteen forty-two, four Japanese carriers and the USS Yorktown went down. |
| 9.35-14.35 | Drain the Pacific, and there's one, as far from Midway as Boston is from New York. |
| (0.5 s) | |
| 14.85-17.60 | More than three miles down, it's still sitting upright. |
| 17.95-19.75 | Two more are down there somewhere. |
| (0.85 s hold) | |
| 20.60-23.50 | The carriers never even saw each other. |
| 23.80-26.05 | They fought with planes, from far out at sea. |
| (0.3 s) | |
| 26.35-26.92 | That's why |
| (loop) | the carriers sunk at Midway aren't at Midway. |

**Pronunciation notes for Rodney**
- No Hawaiian word is voiced in this Short.
- Midway MID-way; USS you-ess-ess; Yorktown YORK-town; Japanese jap-uh-NEEZ; Pacific puh-SIF-ik; Boston BAW-stun; New York noo YORK. "nineteen forty-two" spelled out so it is read as a year; "In June of" avoids the comma pause.
- Description only (not voiced): Papahānaumokuākea pah-pah-HAH-now-moh-koo-ah-KEH-ah (lexicon); Hawaiʻi hah-WHY-ee, with a glottal catch before the final "ee".
- Ship names (never voiced): Kaga KAH-gah; Akagi ah-KAH-ghee (hard g); Sōryū SOH-ryoo; Hiryū HEE-ryoo.

**Fallback if VERIFY fails on "The carriers never even saw each other."** Use "The carriers stayed far apart." (true: their strikes flew to targets far beyond sight), read at 20.9-22.6; then "They fought with planes," at 22.9 and "from far out at sea." ending at 25.15; then "That's why" at 25.45-26.0, with T at about 26.1. Shots 7-10 compress by 0.9 s.

**Audio master:** -14 LUFS integrated, VO at least 12 LU above the bed; exactly 3 sonar pings; 2 disc taps; the paper postcard motif twice (peel, drop); no SFX motif more than 3 times. Master as a loop: render T + 2 s and fold the tail onto the head. Test by playing 26.0-27.0 and 0.0-1.0 back to back on a phone speaker.

### 3. Hook

- VO at 0.04: "The carriers sunk at Midway aren't at Midway." (8 words, about 2.9 s, word for word the title.) The contradiction opens "then where are they?", which the next 14 s answer. The take puts this sentence mid-paragraph, for a lifted, non-final read.
- Frame 0 is already moving and carries the question with the sound off: a low golden-morning oblique from inside Midway's lagoon looks northwest across turquoise water and the white surf of the outer reef to an empty horizon; a thick yellow line is already racing out from the reef up the centre; an off-white postcard (MIDWAY / ATOLL) in the upper third with a tucked-in paper slip carrying one ink carrier silhouette. "ATOLL" plus the reef reads as a place (not the USS Midway museum ship); the carrier reads as war, not travel.
- At 1.60, on "aren't", the carrier slip peels off the card and skims out along the line to the horizon, vanishing into the head dot as "Midway" ends (2.85): the paradox is seen as well as heard by about 2.9 s.
- No clone device (no black caption box, curved arrow, depth label, dashed sea-level line or tan basin); not Short 4's top-down ring; no Everest, no "taller than", no title clone words.
- Premise check (ytsearch, 2026-11-08; short05/probe.txt, probe2.txt, probe3.txt): the exact angle returns 0 on-angle Shorts; "how deep are the midway wrecks" finds wreck long-forms at 140K-613K but no Short on where the wrecks are; Midway Shorts pull 1.5M-5.1M.

### 4. Payoff

Four beats, each landing on a picture; only one number shown big.
1. **Where (9.35-14.35).** "Drain the Pacific" starts the drain on the word, so it reads as a thought experiment, not history. The camera pulls out to a 120 km high oblique where the line runs straight up the frame from Midway's limestone crown to a dot at Kaga's recorded position. "...and there's one, as far from Midway as Boston is from New York." A true-size paper cut-out of the Northeast coast lies down on the drained floor, Boston at Midway and New York at the dot: US viewers feel the 190 miles without hearing the number.
2. **Depth (14.45-16.05).** The camera whip-dives down the line to the old sea surface, then falls straight down beside a yellow plumb that holds still while the world rushes up: the whole 9:16 frame becomes the gauge. MORE THAN / 3 MILES / DOWN stamps on, 3 and MILES together; the second ping hits as the plumb touches bottom.
3. **Upright (16.3-17.6).** An inset opens on a plain hull standing upright, buried in sediment: "it's still sitting upright." A war-grave image with no wreck footage and no debris.
4. **The count and the why (17.95-26.92).** The five discs return; on "down there" the ink drains out of two of them and SOMEWHERE appears; a third ping goes out and gets no echo (the emotional peak, held 0.85 s). The sea rises back over the grave; the camera climbs to the same high oblique with the ocean restored. "The carriers never even saw each other." (a real "huh" late in the Short). "They fought with planes, from far out at sea": three paper planes fly the whole line toward Midway, and the camera follows them home. "That's why..." completes the hook on the replay.

Tone throughout: war graves. No music, no gags, no treasure framing, no real wreck imagery.

### 5. Loop check

**Audio.** "The carriers never even saw each other. They fought with planes, from far out at sea. That's why the carriers sunk at Midway aren't at Midway. In June of nineteen forty-two, four Japanese carriers..." "That's why" plus the hook is one ordinary English sentence; heard cold, the hook is a plain statement. Both pieces come from one take, cut at a zero crossing in the middle of the 80-150 ms gap between "why" and "the", so the replay plays the take's own continuous audio with its real breath; the gap also absorbs YouTube's small loop hitch. Both sides of the seam have mid-paragraph prosody; "sea" has a continuing-intonation variant (V2) and the beat before "That's why" is 0.3 s, so nothing sounds like THE END. The drone bed is a seamless 27.000 s loop; the surf ends at its frame-0 level; the mix is loop-mastered.

**Picture.** The last frame (809, t 26.967) is frame 0's camera and state one frame earlier: pose C0 (on the 323° line, 2.5 km inside the NW reef crest, 700 m up, heading 323°, pitch -15°, vertical FOV 62°); same sun; same lagoon, surf and horizon; the same MIDWAY ATOLL card with the carrier slip tucked in, settled by 26.30. The descent into C0 is a C1 spline whose end conditions equal H(-0.6) in position and velocity; frames 792-809 are rendered from H(t-27.0); the line's rewind and redraw reverse as one velocity zero-crossing at 26.40 (one frame, no hold); the planes left the frame by 25.40; water, foam, glints and the bed run on a periodic 27.000 s clock. The caption flips from THAT'S WHY to THE CARRIERS exactly at the word boundary.

**Seam tests before upload:** frame 809 to frame 0 MAE ≤ about 5/255 and SSIM ≥ 0.97; concatenate the Short with itself and run temporal_scan.py (no "cut" or "flash"); play 26.0-27.0 and 0.0-1.0 back to back (no pop, click or doubled frame); after a private test upload (owner's OK needed even for private or unlisted), check the loop on a real phone.

### 6. Facts

Claim by claim against the brief's fact spine (brief section 4) and the ledger (C5-xx in research/ep02/script/ledger_part3.json).
1. **"The carriers sunk at Midway aren't at Midway."** Spine #15 plus brief must-fix #2: the wrecks lie on the deep ocean floor, not on the atoll; Kaga about 304 km (189 mi) out, Yorktown about 277 km (172 mi) (C5-15).
2. **"In June of nineteen forty-two, four Japanese carriers and the USS Yorktown went down."** Spine #14; C5-12 (all four Japanese fleet carriers present; Yorktown crippled on 4 June, sank 7 June 1942). All five sank in June 1942. Never "Japan lost all its carriers".
3. **"Drain the Pacific, and there's one, as far from Midway as Boston is from New York."** A thought experiment ("Drain..."), with the drain starting on that word (look-book blocker B3). "One" is Kaga: its recorded sinking position, 30°23'N 179°17'W, is 304.3 km / 189.1 mi from Sand Island at a bearing of 323.0° (haversine; C5-15: 304 km / 189 mi). NEW LEDGER ITEM (computed 2026-11-08): Boston City Hall (42.3601N, 71.0589W) to New York City Hall (40.7128N, 74.0060W) is 306.1 km / 190.2 mi great-circle (BOS-JFK 186.3 mi); "as far as" holds to within 1%. Akagi (176 mi) and Yorktown (172 mi) are not compared; the narration says "one".
4. **"More than three miles down,"** C5-14: Kaga and Akagi "found 5,400 meters (more than 17,000 feet) below the surface"; Yorktown 16,650 ft (3.15 mi); "more than three miles down" is true for all three. Brief unit table: 5.4 km = 3.4 mi.
5. **"it's still sitting upright."** NEW LEDGER ITEM: Wikipedia, Japanese aircraft carrier Kaga: "She sits upright", "buried up to the degaussing wires in the seafloor and with most of her superstructure and flight deck missing"; so the inset hull is upright, partly buried, with no island and no flight deck. Nautilus surveyed the wreck in Sept 2023 (spine #15). Re-read the article on upload day.
6. **"Two more are down there somewhere."** TRUE under every scenario. Sōryū and Hiryū sank in June 1942 (C5-12), so their wrecks lie somewhere on the sea floor; C5-13 found no public report of their discovery as of 2026-11-08 and bans "still lost" and "never found" (absence of a report is not proof). This line claims neither, and no position is drawn for them. Re-check the news on upload day.
7. **"The carriers never even saw each other."** VERIFY (not in the spine; add to the ledger). NHHC, "Battle of Midway, 3-6 June 1942, Combat Narrative": "The surface ships in both forces never see each other; all attacks are by air." (quoted by the search engine on 2026-11-08; the page returned 503). Air & Space Forces Magazine, "The Battle of Midway": "At Midway, the opposing fleets never came within sight of each other." "Carriers" rather than "fleets" because submarine I-168 did sight Yorktown. Never say "first" (Coral Sea came first). Read the NHHC page before lock; if it fails, use the fallback line.
8. **"They fought with planes, from far out at sea."** VERIFY-light (standard history): land-based planes from Midway and carrier planes attacked Nagumo's force; Spruance launched "at extreme range" (Wikipedia, Battle of Midway). It describes how the carriers fought, not what finished each ship (a submarine finished Yorktown; Japanese destroyers scuttled burning carriers). C5-10: the carrier fight happened at sea, far from the atoll.
9. **"That's why the carriers sunk at Midway aren't at Midway."** Follows from 3, 7 and 8.

**Visual facts**
- The dot is at Kaga's recorded sinking position (the description says "about"). HO projections: Midway (549, 1158), Kaga (552, 542), Kure (176, 886). Akagi's position would fall at about (752, 566) but is not drawn: Akagi and Yorktown are counted only in the disc row (no fake positions). Pearl and Hermes is out of frame.
- Kure is 92 km from Sand Island at 283°; it gets its own reef crown, dimmed (spine #13).
- Midway's crown on a drowned basalt volcano follows spines #11-12; the crown band is schematic (limestone above about -400 m).
- The Boston-New York cut-out is a real coastline (Natural Earth, public domain) at true scale, rotated 88.8° (Boston-NYC bearing 234.2° onto the line's 323.0°); its dots land within about 2 km of the line ends.
- Disc order Akagi, Kaga, Sōryū, Hiryū, then Yorktown: discs 1, 2 and 5 stay solid; discs 3 and 4 lose their ink (spine #15, C5-13).
- Plane silhouettes are home-built, generic and unmarked; no narration says whose they are; consistent with the Japanese air strike on Midway on 4 June, but an illustration only.
- Hull and plane art home-built; no OET, NOAA or Vulcan imagery, no flags, no gore, no treasure framing. War-grave framing: C5-16 (NHHC Director Cox: "important war graves of both American and Japanese sailors").
- The plunge depth uses real ETOPO/GEBCO depth at Kaga's position (expect about 5.4 km). The C0 reef-crest point is approximate; set it on the imagery.
- Yorktown photo VERIFY: best NHHC catalogue match 80-G-17062 ("dead in the water and listing heavily", afternoon of 4 June 1942, before abandonment); a U.S. Navy photo is public domain. Confirm it is the same file the long-form uses and that no casualties are visible (alternatives 80-G-21666, 80-G-414423).

Dropped or avoided: the "AF is short of water" decoy, Everest, 56,000 ft, the dent, any Hawaiian name in the narration, on-screen sources, "three have been found", "never found", and the long-form's own wreck line.

### 7. Renders: new for this Short vs reused

New, Short-only, vertical 1080x1920 at 30 fps. The long-form and its files are not touched; every changed asset is a copy.
1. **Pose C0 and the hook motion H(t)** for t from -0.6 to 3.15 s; the seam frames 792-809 rendered from H(t-27.0); re-bake shadows and AO for the morning sun (azimuth 70, elevation 14) if the long-form used another sun. The lagoon foreground needs the sharpest drape available (Sentinel-2 10 m or Landsat 15 m pan); if soft, raise C0 to about 1.0 km and re-aim so the reef surf sits at y ≈ 980.
2. **Shot 2:** the rise and pull-back from C0 to pose A over the live sea, with the line drawn on the water surface to the horizon.
3. **Shot 3:** the drain on "Drain" (0 to -5,400 m over 9.40-12.40) with a log pull-back to HO; the line drapes by z = max(terrain, water level); the head dot stops at Kaga's recorded position.
4. **Regional terrain:** GEBCO 2024 15" over lat 27.0-31.5, lon -180.0 to -175.5 (does not cross the antimeridian). Materials: pelagic-clay browns below about -4,500 m; basalt on the volcanoes; neutral pale limestone crowns on Midway and Kure (Kure dimmed to 60%, new mask). gate.py on frames 0.0, 7.5, 11.9, 15.0, 16.2 and 24.0 (tan ≤ 10%; log the blue share). Mean luma ≥ 70 per shot.
5. **True-size paper cut-out** (new asset): Natural Earth or TIGER (public domain) Massachusetts-Rhode Island-Connecticut-New York coastline, Boston to New York City, true scale, rotated 88.8°, rendered as off-white paper at 85% with an ink coast and two city dots, projected flat onto the sampled floor; slide in, then peel away.
6. **Shot 4 dive and plunge:** a 0.45 s log-altitude whip-dive from HO down the line to P1 with motion blur, then a straight drop to P2. The plumb is a 3D vertical line from z 0 to the floor, projected and drawn crisp in the UI layer ((713, 273) to (744, 1403) at P2); the falling dot synced to the camera's depth fraction; the turquoise wave tick a new glyph.
7. **Shot 7 refill:** the camera rides 300 m above the rising water, pitch +13° to -16°, heading 316° to 323°; the plumb consumed by the rising water; the inset closes as the water covers the hull.
8. **Shot 8:** the climb from 300 m back to HO over the restored sea, translation weighted to high altitude, motion blur at a 180° shutter; peak feature motion ≤ 90 px/frame (check with temporal_scan.py).
9. **Shot 9:** three home-built paper plane silhouettes fly down the line with soft water shadows; the log descent from HO into C0 as a C1 spline ending at H(-0.6) in position and velocity, while the line rewinds.
10. **New UI pieces:** MIDWAY ATOLL postcard (two lines) with a removable carrier slip (peels at 1.60, drops back in at 25.90); the MIDWAY card (cap 80); five paper discs with an ink-drain-to-dashed transition; the depth card MORE THAN / 3 MILES / DOWN with stamp-in; SOMEWHERE in ink with a halo; BOSTON and NEW YORK labels; the inset zoom circle (iris open and close); the house caption track.
11. **Hull art:** a copy of the long-form's honest wreck-line hull silhouette, trimmed to a plain hull with no island and no flight deck, lower part buried in sediment.
12. **Shot 2 print:** a 780 px paper-print treatment of the existing Yorktown photo (x 96-876, tilt +2°, push 1.00 to 1.03), sliding up in and sinking out (a print rather than full-bleed because a 9:16 crop would cut the hull).
13. **Audio:** loop-mastered mix on a 27.000 s drone bed; three sonar pings (soft; deep with an echo; no echo); drain rumble and refill swell, surf, faint engine hum; paper peel and drop (postcard motif), two disc taps, a paper drag; the VO cut at the zero crossing in the middle of the "why | the" gap.

QC sheets: a 390 px phone sheet of every shot's middle frame; a typeqc log (every cap ≥ 77 px; all text inside x 72-849, y 200-1529, captions per house spec); the seam MAE/SSIM and self-concatenation scan.

**Reused as-is from the long-form:** the NHHC Yorktown public-domain photo file and its grade (Ch5); the Midway terrain data, drape, water shader, foam rim and drain level curve (the reef ring and drain/reef cap), only retimed and re-cammed; the yellow line and head-dot style, the Anton and Barlow type system and the paper style; the music-down tone of the wreck beat.

### 8. Title, description, link

**Title:** The Carriers Sunk at Midway Aren't at Midway (44 characters; passes the niche title regex and the clone-word ban; word for word the spoken hook; exact-title probe found 0 on-angle matches). Trade-off: "Midway" is word 5, not in the first 3 words; fallback "Midway's Sunken Carriers Aren't at Midway" (41, also passes) loses the "sunk at / aren't at" mirror.

**Release:** D+9. Related Video set to "Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down)". Nothing is uploaded, scheduled or made visible without the owner's explicit OK.

**Description** (no emojis; sources only here):

> The Battle of Midway is named for the atoll, but the carriers sunk in it lie far out on the open Pacific floor. Kaga rests about 190 miles from Midway, roughly as far as Boston is from New York, more than three miles down and still upright. Akagi and USS Yorktown have also been found. No discovery of Sōryū or Hiryū has been reported. The wrecks are war graves of Japanese and American sailors, inside Papahānaumokuākea Marine National Monument.
>
> Watch the full film, with the ocean around Hawaiʻi drained away: Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down). It is linked above.
>
> Approximate depths and distances:
> - Kaga about 5.4 km (more than 17,000 ft) down, about 190 miles from Midway.
> - Akagi more than 17,000 ft down.
> - USS Yorktown about 5.1 km (16,650 ft) down, about 172 miles from Midway.
> - Boston to New York City is about 190 miles in a straight line.
> Seafloor from GEBCO and NOAA ETOPO 2022; coastline from Natural Earth. The ship and plane shapes and the coastline cut-out are our own simple drawings, not images of the wrecks.
>
> Sources:
> - Naval History and Heritage Command, Battle of Midway, 3-6 June 1942, Combat Narrative
> - Air & Space Forces Magazine, "The Battle of Midway"
> - Ocean Exploration Trust, Nautilus Live, "Exploring Iconic Shipwrecks of the Battle of Midway" (Sept 2023)
> - Papahānaumokuākea Marine National Monument, Kaga discovery (Oct 2019)
> - MIT News, Yorktown found (June 1998)
> - Wikipedia: Battle of Midway; Japanese aircraft carrier Kaga; Japanese aircraft carrier Akagi; USS Yorktown (CV-5)
>
> Photo: USS Yorktown, 4 June 1942, U.S. Navy / Naval History and Heritage Command, public domain. [Catalogue number after VERIFY.]

Integrator note: the Akagi depth uses the ledger's C5-14 wording ("more than 17,000 feet", Papahānaumokuākea), since the ledger gives no Akagi-specific depth beyond that.

**Pinned comment** (a place with living memory): "Did someone in your family serve at Midway, on either side? Tell us their story." Heart and answer the family stories; no jokes in replies.

**Tags:** Battle of Midway, Midway Atoll, USS Yorktown, Kaga, Akagi, WW2 shipwrecks, Pacific Ocean floor, drained ocean, Hawaii, Papahānaumokuākea. "Drained" is allowed in tags, not in the title.

**How it links to the long-form:** it rebuilds the long-form's Ch5 Midway elements as a solemn myth-bust (the reef ring, the drain and reef cap, the NHHC Yorktown photo, the honest wreck line with the home-built hull) and adds Short-only devices (the postcard carrier slip, the Boston-New York true-size cut-out, the vertical plunge gauge, the ink-draining discs). It never spends the long-form's payoff (no 56,000 ft, no dent, no Everest, no "taller than"). The long-form is unchanged.

### 9. Panel must-fixes and how this version answers them

Panel score on the draft: 68. All 9 must-fixes are in: the drain starts on "Drain the Pacific" with the 1942 sentence on the live reef; the "three ... three" collision is gone (no spoken count of found wrecks; 3 and MILES land together); WHERE is answered with the Boston-New York cut-out and no second big number; "fleets" became "carriers" (VERIFY); the beat before "That's why" is 0.3 s with a continuing-intonation variant; the hook sentence sits mid-paragraph; the palette is tan-free; Kure has a dimmed reef crown; frame 0 carries a war signal (the carrier slip), with the plumb at x 713-744 and the print 780 px wide.

## Short 7 (S12-almost-twice-everest + P2-01-cropped, with the P2-04 beat): "This Beach Is on a Mountain Almost Twice Everest"

| | |
|---|---|
| Release | **D+11**, same clock hour as the long-form (slot 7 of 7; never before D+7) |
| Length | L = 27.40 s planned (822 frames); recompute from the real timing.json |
| The one big number | ABOUT 56,000 FEET (the only Short allowed to spend the long-form's payoff) |
| Voice | Rodney - Clear Educator, `eY2VJs4Gi8QKG2RV01sS`, `eleven_v4`, passed explicitly |
| Format | 1080x1920, 30 fps, VE 1.0; TerrainStage curvature on |
| Related Video | "Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down)" |

### Integrator changes (2026-11-08)

1. **Two long-form lines rewritten** (the owner wants Short narration written new):
   - k06 "Like a bowling ball on a mattress." (verbatim long-form s164) became **"Bowling ball, meet mattress."** The metaphor stays (it is a long-form element); the line is new and deadpan.
   - k07 "Measured from its true base, Mauna Loa is about fifty-six thousand feet tall." (verbatim s169) became **"From its true base, Mauna Loa stands about fifty-six thousand feet tall."** The ledger qualifier "from its true base" (C6-06) stays word for word on purpose.
   - Retime: from k06 on, events moved earlier (about -0.5 s through "tall", -0.8 s after it); L went from 28.20 to 27.40 s. Every anchor is re-derived from the real timing.json anyway.
2. **Burned-in captions added** in the house style (shorts_plan.md section 4), suppressed while the SEA FLOOR tag sits near the caption band (8.05-10.25) and while the number card shows ABOUT / 56,000 / FEET. The writer's reason for no captions (the accent-word ban) does not apply: the ban is on a black caption box with an accent word, and the house captions are unboxed. Our own SRT is uploaded too.
3. **Known kinship with Short 1, accepted:** both end on an oblivious beach creature ("And he has no idea" / "Somebody tell that turtle:") and both titles say "mountain". They are 11 days apart; Short 1 owns position (more than halfway up) and the moat, this Short owns height (56,000 ft) and the true base.

### 1. Narration and TTS

**TTS input.** engine/tools/tts.py segments with voice `eY2VJs4Gi8QKG2RV01sS` ("Rodney - Clear Educator"), passed explicitly (never the Brian default), model `eleven_v4`, the locked ep02 settings, `lead_in` 0. Markers use the `{m:}` syntax of ep02 tts_spec.json. Respellings go in the voice text only.

```
k01  This {m:beach}beach is on a {m:mountain}mountain almost {m:twice}twice as tall as {m:ev1}Everest.   [pause 0.30]
k02  It's {m:loa}MAO-nah LOH-ah, and most of this mountain is {m:under}underwater.   [pause 0.30]
k03  From the {m:sea}sea floor, it already beats {m:ev2}Everest.   [pause 0.40]
k04  But that's not the {m:bottom}bottom.   [pause 0.40]
k05  It's so {m:heavy}heavy, it {m:pushed}pushed the sea floor down by {m:miles}miles.   [pause 0.15]
k06  {m:bowl}Bowling {m:ball}ball, meet {m:mattress}mattress.   [pause 0.75]
k07  {m:from}From its {m:truebase}true base, MAO-nah LOH-ah stands {m:about}about fifty-six {m:thousand}thousand {m:feet}feet {m:tall}tall.   [speed 0.95, pause 0.95]
k08  Somebody tell that {m:turtle}turtle:   [pause to the file end, about 0.27 s]
```

**As heard** (66 words, about 27.4 s):
> This beach is on a mountain almost twice as tall as Everest. It's Mauna Loa, and most of this mountain is underwater. From the sea floor, it already beats Everest. But that's not the bottom. It's so heavy, it pushed the sea floor down by miles. Bowling ball, meet mattress. From its true base, Mauna Loa stands about fifty-six thousand feet tall. Somebody tell that turtle:

**Estimated times** (Rodney's measured ep02 rate: 153 wpm median within segments; s164 took 2.56 s; s169 took 5.76 s at 0.9x). Retime every anchor from the real timing.json.

| Seg | Time |
|---|---|
| k01 | 0.06-4.25 |
| k02 | 4.55-7.85 |
| k03 | 8.15-10.85 |
| k04 | 11.25-12.95 |
| k05 | 13.35-17.05 |
| k06 | 17.20-18.90 |
| k07 | 19.65-24.83 ("about" ~21.90, "thousand" ~22.95, "feet" ~23.38) |
| k08 | 25.78-27.13 |
| L | 27.40 s |

**Why the hook is clean:** the loop line is a separate clause ending on a colon. On the replay, the hook is the message the turtle needs to hear, so it is a complete, stressed, falling declarative that starts its own take, not a mid-clause splice (the playbook's proven "...tell them this:" pattern).

**Loop takes** (needs a small, default-off tts.py change):
- Add a spec flag `"loop": true`. When set, the last segment's next_text becomes k01's text, and k01's previous_text becomes k08's text. Default off, so no other spec (including the long-form's) changes behaviour.
- Make 3 takes of k01 with the wrap and 3 "fresh" takes (no previous_text), and 3 takes of k08 (bump `"take"`).
- Pick k01 by a cold test: play 0.0-2.0 s on a phone speaker; "THIS beach" must be stressed and sound like a new statement. Pick k08 for an open, slightly rising end on "turtle".
- Test the chosen pair in both directions: five loops of "turtle: | This" on a phone; loudness matched within 0.5 LU short-term; no pitch jump beyond a normal new-sentence reset.
- asr_check.py: "MAO-nah LOH-ah" must come back as "Mauna Loa" (lexicon asr_accept); "mattress" and "fifty-six thousand" as scripted.

**Pronunciation**
- Mauna Loa is the only Hawaiian name spoken (twice). Voice text uses the lexicon's "MAO-nah LOH-ah" (lexicon id mauna_loa): voiced in the locked long-form (s067, s165, s169) and passed the ASR check on 2026-11-08. "Mau" rhymes with "cow"; "Loa" has two syllables (LOH-ah), never one. IPA /ˈmaʊ.nə ˈloʊ.ə/; Hawaiian [ˈmɐwnə ˈlowə]. Never "Mow-nuh" (the lexicon warns "ow" can be read /moʊ/).
- Hawaiian words in writing only (notes in case one is ever voiced): Hawaiʻi plain "Hawaii" or hah-WHY-ee with a light glottal catch before the final "ee"; Punaluʻu poo-nah-LOO-oo, glottal stop before the last "oo" (rule-derived, not in the lexicon; VERIFY on wehewehe.org); Kaʻū kah-OO, glottal stop and a long final "oo" (rule-derived; VERIFY); honu HOH-noo.
- Lexicon open item 1 (a person checks wehewehe.org) still applies to Mauna Loa before the final mix.

### 2. Second-by-second table

**Poses.** All at VE 1.0. The shot axis runs at bearing 164.7° from Mauna Loa's summit (19.475 N, 155.603 W) through Punaluʻu (19.1361 N, 155.5047 W); the camera always faces back up the axis, heading 344.7°. TerrainStage curvature is on.
- A "Aerial" (frame 0 = last frame): 3.0 km off the beach at 19.1101 N, 155.4971 W; alt 600 m, pitch -3.0°, vfov 34°; computed with curvature: summit skyline at y ≈ 538, beach at y ≈ 1417.
- M "Massif" (mid-dive, about 5.0 s): about 80 km from the summit, alt about -1.0 km, pitch about -2°, vfov about 16°; the whole drained dome in frame (green top at y ≈ 320-577, bathtub ring at y ≈ 577, dark flank to the bottom).
- S "Section": camera 150 km down the axis at 18.1753 N, 155.2259 W; alt -3.5 km (1.56 km above the local floor, ETOPO -5,057 m); pitch 0.0°, vfov 6.0°, 8.19 m/px; summit edge at y 240, in-plane 0 m line at y 749, sea-floor band at y 1360. The cut plane is vertical, running ENE-WSW (74.7°/254.7°) through the summit.
- D "Deep": the same camera at vfov 6.0°; pitch -2.8° puts the band at y 472 before the dent; pitch -4.9° puts the true base at y 760 after it.
- H "Hero": the same camera at vfov 11.5° (15.73 m/px), pitch -2.0°; summit edge at y 250, true base at y 1340, Everest's peak at y 778 (51.6%).
- All section moves are tilts and zooms of one camera; it never enters rock, even when the cut closes; foreshortening ≤ 0.4%. Check every pixel target on the render.

**Layout.** Text at x 74-849, y 200-1500 (captions per house spec), caps ≥ 80 px (widths measured on the engine's fonts). Style: ink #1E1A16 on paper #F6F1E4, 2-4 px deckled edge, soft shadow (S11 paper grammar); no plates, scrims, black boxes, arrows, dashed lines or accent colours. Summit point at x 540. Tape: a 32 px paper strip at x 524-556, ink ticks every 1,000 m, no numbers. Number card hangs from the tape head on its left, x 74-524, 408 px tall: ABOUT (Barlow Condensed 800, cap 80, 277 px wide), 56,000 (Anton, cap 132, 417 px), FEET (Barlow Condensed 800, cap 88, 224 px). Everest: the SRTM summit on a plain grey pedestal, footprint 2.2 km, 5 px white outline, standing in the cut plane 50 m in front of the face, centred 1.6 km ENE of the summit; on screen x 600-870 at 8.19 m/px, x 572-712 at 15.73 m/px.

**Captions:** house spec; suppressed 8.05-10.25 (the SEA FLOOR tag at y 1372-1482 sits too close to the band) and 21.90-24.83 (the number card shows ABOUT / 56,000 / FEET); none on frame 0 (the first card appears at 0.06) and none after 27.13.

**0.00-0.95**
- Picture: POSE A; frame 0 = the last frame. A new aerial plate on the long-form's Big Island terrain. Sky (deep blue to pale haze) fills y 0-530; Mauna Loa's broad dome on the skyline at y ≈ 538-590, above green-brown Kaʻū slopes; a black-sand crescent with its palm grove at y ≈ 1400-1430, about 525 px wide; turquoise water and rolling surf to the bottom edge. A giant paper honu standee basks on the sand (x 360-800, y 1150-1417): a cut-out of a public-domain NOAA or USFWS photo, warm paper edge, base tab, contact shadow, clearly paper and giant. Frame 0 is a wave's run-up crest, foam at the honu's base tab, velocity 0. From frame 0 the backwash pulls the foam sheet down the sand; the whole sea starts sinking (0 to -25 m by 0.95, ease-in). The surf keeps rolling and the honu's edge flutters on the loop clock. The camera eases away from rest.
- Text: POSTCARD at x 90-846, y 200-512, tilt -3°, deckled edge: "ALMOST TWICE" (Anton cap 96), "EVEREST" (Anton cap 130), a blank stamp with no words at x 740-830, y 400-490. Captions from 0.06: THIS BEACH / IS ON A.
- VO: VO1 "This beach is on a" (from 0.06)

Dark basalt (#2B2927-#3A3532), olive-brown sediment drapes and drowned reef steps rise as a widening band below the coast. ### 3. Hook

- VO from 0.06 s: "This beach is on a mountain almost twice as tall as Everest." (12 words, ending at 4.25 s; the paradox "This beach is on a mountain" is spoken by about 1.4 s). The sentence is complete and starts its own take; on the replay it is the message the last line asks for ("Somebody tell that turtle:"), so it opens as a stressed, falling statement.
- Frame 0 = the last frame: POSE A, a 600 m aerial oblique 3 km off Punaluʻu, looking up the slope at Mauna Loa: turquoise surf on a black-sand crescent; a giant paper honu basking on the sand (plainly a paper cut-out of a real photo); green Kaʻū slopes climbing to Mauna Loa's broad dome on the skyline; the off-white postcard "ALMOST TWICE / EVEREST" in the sky, 3 words that read in about 1 s with the sound off.
- In the swipe window: frame 0 is a wave at its run-up crest, the backwash starts at once and the whole sea begins to sink; on "mountain" (0.95 s) the ocean plunges, a white foam rim races away from the beach and a dark basalt flank rises below the coast; at 2.55 s the postcard tips into the falling sea. By 2 s a famous turtle beach has visibly lost its ocean: not travel footage.
- The question: a beach sits at sea level, so how can it be on a mountain almost twice Everest? The picture starts answering on "mountain".

### 4. Payoff

**The climb and the number (19.65-23.30).** On "From" a paper measuring tape starts unrolling from the TRUE BASE tag, at the bottom of the dent. The camera climbs the sliced face; the tape head climbs with it, carrying the ink-on-paper number card. At about 21.85 the tape head passes Everest's peak side by side with the counter near 29,000: the viewer feels Everest left behind before the number lands. "ABOUT" stamps on with "about"; "56,000" lands on "thousand" as the tape head clicks onto Mauna Loa's summit; "FEET" stamps on with "feet".

**The proof frame and the gag (23.30-25.35).** On "feet tall" the camera pulls back to the hero frame: the whole column from the true base to the summit, the tape running its full height, and Everest on the same base reaching 52%. 0.3 s after "tall" a second Everest drops onto the first with a thunk, its tip just 34 px above the summit edge: "almost twice", proved on screen with no second number, and a laugh. The 56,000 card stays on screen, landed, for about 2.4 s.

**The button (25.35-27.40).** 0.95 s after "tall" a 1.0 s comic snap-back runs: the cut slams shut, both Everests topple out, the camera crash-zooms up the axis while the sea rushes back, and it lands on the giant paper turtle, unmoved, as Rodney says "Somebody tell that turtle:". First-time viewers get a finished joke; the replay delivers the message.

**Funnel note:** the one payoff Short allowed to spend the 56,000-ft true base. Keep the hold (never before D+7; planned D+11). The long-form is not changed.

### 5. Loop check

**Lines, as one passage:** "Somebody tell that turtle: this beach is on a mountain almost twice as tall as Everest." Plain English: a colon introducing a reported message. The hook is a complete sentence in its own take, so it works cold and as the continuation. k08 is generated with k01 as next_text, so it ends open; k01 can carry k08 as previous_text, and the fresh takes are kept for the cold test. Seam silence about 0.33 s (a colon pause): 0.27 s after "turtle" plus about 0.06 s before "This".

**Frames.** The last frame (821, at 27.367 s) and frame 0 share the same camera (POSE A: 19.1101 N, 155.4971 W, 600 m, heading 344.7°, pitch -3.0°, vfov 34°), the same POST grade and sun, and the same state: the sea at 0 m with a wave at its run-up crest at the honu's base tab and water velocity 0; surf and honu flutter on a loop clock (surf period L/5 = 5.48 s, flutter L/10 = 2.74 s); the honu at rest and the postcard settled (from 26.90) in the same place; no tags, card, tape or Everest; the cut closed; no caption (the last card is gone at 27.13; the first appears at 0.06).

**Motion.** Position, heading, pitch and vfov all reach velocity 0 at 27.40 (the snap-back decelerates from vfov 30° to 34° and to rest) and all ease away from rest at 0.00, so there is no lens-breathing gap. The camera turns around like a pendulum at the seam (arrives moving toward the coast, leaves easing back down the axis); the water does the same (rises to the crest, then backwashes). Every channel sits at a natural hang point.

**Audio.** The surf bed is one seamless loop of exactly L, tail crossfaded into head, the run-up wash on the seam. VO edges get 5-8 ms fades. Music is silent from 26.20 through 0.40. No SFX crosses the seam.

**After the TTS retime:** recompute L; set the surf period to L/5 and the flutter period to L/10; re-cut the surf bed to exactly L; re-key the run-up crest to t = 0 ≡ L.

**QC:** first/last-frame MAE ≤ 5/255; concatenate the Short with itself and run temporal_scan.py (no "cut" or "flash"); loop it 5 times on a phone speaker and listen at "turtle: | This" for any click, loudness step or pitch jump; play 0.0-2.0 s cold on a phone to check the hook's prosody.

### 6. Facts

Every claim is inside spine #2 and #3 or the ep02 ledger. No fact VERIFY is open; the open items are assets and lexicon only.
1. **"This beach is on a mountain"** (Punaluʻu Black Sand Beach, 19.1361 N, 155.5047 W). VERIFIED: the USGS Geologic Map of the Island of Hawaiʻi (Wolfe and Morris 1996), served by the Hawaiʻi Statewide GIS layer "Geologic Units", gives volcano "mloa" here: Kaʻū Basalt, Qk1y, 3,000-5,000 yr (point-queried by the writer and re-queried by the panel on 2026-11-08). Kīlauea's Puna Basalt begins about 1 km NE (19.145 N, 155.495 W); at 3 km out, POSE A frames only ±0.5 km of shore, west of that contact.
2. **Hook and postcard "almost twice as tall as Everest".** Spine #2: about 17,170 m (56,000 ft) from the true base; spine #3: Everest 8,849 m (29,032 ft); ratio 1.94 (ledger C6-07). The narration says "almost" and names Mauna Loa every time.
3. **"It's Mauna Loa, and most of this mountain is underwater."** Spine #2 (USGS Volcano Watch, "How high is Mauna Loa?"): 4,170 m above sea level and 5,000 m below to the sea floor, so 55% is under water measured from the sea floor (76% from the true base). Wording per C3-02 ("mountain" or "height", never volume) and C12-01. The section draws it true: 55% of the column below the in-plane 0 m line.
4. **"From the sea floor, it already beats Everest."** Arithmetic on the same Volcano Watch figures: 4,170 + 5,000 = 9,170 m (30,080 ft) against 8,849 m, a 3.6% margin. ETOPO 2022 along this axis (computed 2026-11-08): -5,057 m at 150 km, -5,084 m at 130-140 km; the claim holds for any sea-floor depth beyond about 4,680 m. ADD to the ledger: "C6-xx Mauna Loa from the sea floor vs Everest", verified (arithmetic on a primary source).
5. **"But that's not the bottom."** Spine #2 "from its true base"; ledger C6-04.
6. **"It's so heavy, it pushed the sea floor down by miles."** Spine #2: the sea floor "depressed ... another 8,000 m"; the Moho "bowed downward by as much as 8 km". C6-05: "by miles" is safe. No number in the animation; the band sinks almost level in the 9 km frame (no false bowl); the shape is carried by the metaphor.
7. **"Bowling ball, meet mattress."** The long-form's metaphor (brief Ch6; s164), in new words. No claim.
8. **"From its true base, Mauna Loa stands about fifty-six thousand feet tall."** / ABOUT 56,000 FEET. Spine #2 (USGS Volcano Watch: "total relief of Mauna Loa, from its true base to its summit, is about 17,170 m (56,000 ft)"); ledger C6-06. The USGS Mauna Loa page gives "17 km (55,700 ft)", which "about" also covers. "From its true base" is on screen the whole time (TRUE BASE tag and card joined by the tape). The counter runs on the USGS figures (base -13,000 m, summit +4,169 m), scaled by 0.3% so it lands exactly on the round figure; it never shows a wrong value.
9. **Picture: Everest on the same base reaches 52%** (8,849 / 17,170), true only with a full-height Everest on a pedestal (C3-03, C6-07). The counter passes about 29,000 at Everest's peak (spine #3, 29,032 ft).
10. **Stack gag:** two Everests (17,698 m) top Mauna Loa (17,170 m) by 528 m = 34 px at 15.73 m/px; a playful prop gag that claims nothing beyond "almost twice".
11. **On-screen tags:** MAUNA LOA (the volcano), SEA FLOOR (-5,000 m, spine #2), EVEREST (the prop), TRUE BASE (the USGS phrase, C6-06); the postcard repeats item 2; no qualifier is dropped.
12. **"Somebody tell that turtle:"** A joke. Honu basking at Punaluʻu is documented (Wikipedia, Punaluʻu Beach) but not narrated.

**Staging fact behind the design:** ETOPO 2022 shows a ridge 100-110 km down the axis (-3,770 m on the axis, -2,100 m 10 km WSW). With the engine's curvature it hides everything at the summit plane below about +130 m from the draft's POSE F, so both Everest comparisons sit in the cut plane, where curvature cannot change the 39 px margin.

**Still open (assets and lexicon):** VERIFY that the turtle photo is public domain (a federal-employee credit line from NOAA Fisheries or USFWS, not a permit holder's); if none passes, use the S12 paper sunbather and change VO8 to "Somebody tell that sunbather:". VERIFY a PD or CC0 bowling-ball photo, or model a glossy ball with finger holes and print it on the card. Use only public-domain coastal DEM and ortho (NCEI, USGS 3DEP, NOAA or USGS orthos). The lexicon's wehewehe.org check for Mauna Loa. Add Punaluʻu and Kaʻū to the lexicon before any voicing.

### 7. Renders: new for this Short vs reused

New, for this Short only. Every shot is a new 1080x1920 camera pass; no frame is cut from the long-form, and the long-form does not change.
1. **POSE A aerial plate:** 600 m up, 3 km off Punaluʻu. Terrain: the NCEI Hawaiʻi coastal DEM plus USGS 3DEP 10 m, blended into the HMRG/ETOPO Big Island terrain. Drape: a 0.5-1 m public-domain ortho at the coast. Water: turquoise with surf, a run-up crest and backwash, all on the L/5 loop clock (shared periodic water, shorts_plan.md 5B). Sky gradient deep blue to pale haze. The paper honu standee (cutout.py, card.py; warm paper edge, base tab, contact shadow, flutter on L/10). FRAME-0 GATE: a phone test of the frame-0 still with the teen panel ("real place or game?"); if it fails, go to 1,000 m and 5 km out, re-solve the card and skyline layout, and keep the identical pose at both ends.
2. **The early drain and the dive:** backwash from 0 s, the plunge from 0.95 s, then the dolly-zoom from POSE A through POSE M to POSE S. Anti-clone seabed: dark basalt, olive-brown sediment drapes, reef steps, desaturated red-brown clay; never a pale tan basin. A subtle wet-and-salt bathtub ring at 0 m. No gauge, countdown, dashed line or arrows.
3. **The moving slice:** a vertical ENE-WSW cut sweeping from the camera to the summit plane; the section face from the long-form's Ch6 strata textures, with an in-plane 0 m wet line and a 14 px sediment band; mean luma ≥ 70. The cake cut is look-book upgrade U19, which the long-form's Ch6 also needs: built once in the shared engine (default-off for any scene that does not call it) and used by both; the Short does not change how the long-form uses it.
4. **Everest in the plane:** re-crop the long-form's everest_h16.png to a 2.2 km footprint, pin the summit at 8,849 m above the band, plain grey pedestal, 5 px white outline, 50 m in front of the face; the stage lift, the 2% overshoot, the settle and the hop gag.
5. **The drop under the band, the 8 km dent** (Everest rides down, rock fills in), and the bowling-ball paper inset (a card, a ball cut-out or modelled ball, a quilted paper mattress with a warped dent).
6. **The climb, the hero shot and the stack:** the tilt-up with sub-frame motion blur (look U14; peak about 25 px/frame); the paper tape and the riding number card; the pull-back to POSE H; the second Everest with its dust puff.
7. **The snap-back and the settle:** the cut slam, both Everests falling, the crash move with motion blur; the refill clamped 40 m below the camera; velocity 0 on every channel and a water run-up crest exactly at L.
8. **Type and paper:** the postcard (Anton ink on #F6F1E4, blank stamp); tags in Barlow Condensed 800 (MAUNA LOA, SEA FLOOR, EVEREST, TRUE BASE); the number card (ABOUT / 56,000 / FEET) and the 32 px tape; the house caption track. No black boxes, accent words, yellow, scrims, arrows or dashed lines in the picture layer.
9. **Audio:** a new Rodney VO from tts.py segments with the loop wrap; a new mix at -14 LUFS with VO at least 12 LU over the music; a seamless L-length surf bed; a music edit (the stop on "But" at 11.25, strings at 13.4, a riser and ticks on the climb, a button on the thunk, out by 26.20); comic SFX only on the gags, in the gaps between lines.

**Engine rules** (from lab/drain/terrain.js): TerrainStage curves geometry by d²/2R (1.77 km at 150 km), so Everest, the band, the tape anchor and the slice are in-scene geometry with the same vertex transform; every 2D tag is placed with S.project(), never flat overlay maths; in the section shots the camera stays 1.56 km above the local floor and frames by tilt and zoom only.

**Reused as-is (assets only, no long-form footage):** the HMRG 50 m and ETOPO 2022 Big Island terrain and its bake, with the long-form's sun; the water-plane drain and refill shader; the Ch3 SRTM Everest asset; the Ch6 cut-away strata and the sag curve; the T.slot odometer re-skinned as ink on paper; the shared POST grade; SFX library cues (whooshes, impact_deep, thunk_heavy, tick_counter, paper flap); the long-form's score stems if they suit, otherwise a library cue. Long-form elements it is rebuilt from: Ch3 Everest at the same scale and the Mauna Loa beat; Ch6 the slice, the dent, the bowling ball, the 56,000 counter and Everest on the same base; the refill.

**QC before sign-off:** a phone sheet of every shot's middle frame; the [typeqc] log (every cap ≥ 80 px; at most one block plus one label at a time); luma ≥ 70 per shot; on the 10.10 s frame Everest's peak 30-45 px below the summit edge, and at least 10 px below on the overshoot and hop frames; on the 24.3 s frame Everest reaches 50-54%; on the 25.13 s frame the stack's tip 25-45 px above the summit edge; along the dive and snap-back line the camera at least 1 km above terrain and the water at least 40 m below it; the loop gates above.

### 8. Title, description, link

**Title** (48 characters): This Beach Is on a Mountain Almost Twice Everest. **Backup** (37): Mauna Loa Is Almost Two Everests Tall. Both pass the title regex and the update's clone words (no drained/drain, hidden/hiding, bottom/beneath, nobody, found) and avoid "taller than Everest". Probe 2026-11-08: no Short uses this premise ("this beach is on a mountain": nearest a 20K Short about Salt Lake City's old shoreline; "mauna loa almost twice everest": only "taller/bigger than Everest" Shorts at 0.2K-37K).

**Description:**

> Punaluʻu, the black sand beach on Hawaiʻi's Big Island, sits on the lava of Mauna Loa. Measured from its true base, miles below the sea floor, Mauna Loa is about 56,000 feet tall: almost twice Everest.
> The full film drains the ocean around Hawaiʻi, step by step: Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down) [long-form link]
>
> Sources: USGS Volcano Watch, "How high is Mauna Loa?"; USGS, Mauna Loa volcano page; USGS Geologic Map of the Island of Hawaiʻi (Wolfe and Morris, 1996), via the Hawaiʻi Statewide GIS Program; Mount Everest 8,848.86 m (Nepal-China survey, 2020).
> Data and images: NOAA NCEI ETOPO 2022 and Hawaiʻi coastal DEM; USGS 3DEP; University of Hawaiʻi SOEST HMRG multibeam synthesis; NASA SRTM (Everest); coastal imagery [exact NOAA or USGS ortho credit], public domain; turtle photo [exact NOAA Fisheries or USFWS credit], public domain; bowling ball photo [credit, if a photo is used].
> #Hawaii #MaunaLoa #Everest

**Link and timing:** set the Short's Related Video to "Why Can't You See Most of Hawaii? (Look Down)" (the long-form title stays exactly as approved). Publish no earlier than D+7 (planned D+11), only with the owner's OK. Nothing in the long-form changes.

**Pinned comment:** "Have you seen the turtles at Punaluʻu? Did you know what they were lying on?"

**Pronunciation** of the Hawaiian names written above, in case they are ever voiced: Punaluʻu poo-nah-LOO-oo (VERIFY on wehewehe.org); Hawaiʻi hah-WHY-ee (glottal catch before the last "ee"); Mauna Loa MAO-nah LOH-ah.

**Checks run on this text:** the whole-word AI grep from the playbook (no hits); no emojis; no sources on screen.

### 9. Panel must-fixes and how this version answers them

Panel score on the draft: 77 (the highest of the 7). All 7 must-fixes are in: the loop line is its own clause and the hook a fresh sentence in its own take; the first visual event on frame 0 (backwash) and the plunge on "mountain"; the dead air after the payoff cut (stack 0.3 s after "tall", a 1.0 s snap-back, speech back 0.95 s after "tall"); the number set as ink on a paper card (no yellow, no scrim); both Everest comparisons in the cut plane as in-scene geometry with S.project() tags and measured pixel gates; zero velocity at L on every camera channel and a recomputed loop clock; POSE A as a 600 m aerial with the phone gate kept.
